we can now define our
relational interpretation. As with the set interpretation, the
relational interpretation is given in two parts, in
Definitions
3.2
and
3.2
.

{defi}

The
relational interpretation

\llbracket{\cdot}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}:\mathcal{F}\to[\mathsf{RelEnv},\mathsf{Rel}]
is defined by

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\mathbb{0}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\mathbb{1}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho

\displaystyle=1

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho

\displaystyle=\{\eta:\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\Rightarrow\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\}

\displaystyle=\{(t,t^{\prime})\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{1}\rho)\times\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{2}\rho)~|~

\displaystyle\hskip 21.68121pt\forall{R_{1}:\mathsf{Rel}(A_{1},B_{1})}\,\dots\,{R_{k}:\mathsf{Rel}(A_{k},B_{k})}.

\displaystyle\hskip 28.90755pt(t_{\overline{A}},t^{\prime}_{\overline{B}})\in(\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}])^{\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}\}

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\phi\overline{F}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho

\displaystyle=(\rho\phi)\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho}

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F+G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho+\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F\times G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho\times\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{G}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho

\displaystyle=(\mu T_{H,\rho})\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho}

\displaystyle\text{where }T_{H,\rho}

\displaystyle=(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\pi_{1}\rho},T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\pi_{2}\rho},T^{\mathsf{Rel}}_{H,\rho})

\displaystyle\text{and }T^{\mathsf{Rel}}_{H,\rho}\,F

\displaystyle=\lambda\overline{R}.\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\phi:=F][\overline{\alpha:=R}]

\displaystyle\text{and }T^{\mathsf{Rel}}_{H,\rho}\,\delta

\displaystyle=\lambda\overline{R}.\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathit{id}_{\rho}[\phi:=\delta][\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{\overline{R}}}]

The interpretations in Definitions
3.2

and
3.2
below respect weakening, and also ensure
that the interpretations
\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash F\to G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}
and

\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash\forall\overline{\alpha}.F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}
of the System F arrow
types and
\forall
-types representable in our calculus are as
expected in any parametric model. As for set interpretations,

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}
is

\omega
-cocontinuous; indeed, it is constant (on
\omega
-directed
sets) because Definition
3.2
ensures that restrictions
to
\mathbb{T}^{k}
of morphisms between relational environments are
identities. If
\rho\in\mathsf{RelEnv}
and
\vdash F
, then we write

\llbracket{\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}
instead of
\llbracket{\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho
. For the last

to be well-defined we need

T_{H,\rho}
to be an
\omega
-cocontinuous higher-order relation

, where
k
is the arity of
\phi
, so that, by

1

is

\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}
, this
means that relational interpretations of types must be

\omega
-cocontinuous environment transformer from
\mathsf{RelEnv}
to

RT_{0}
, which in turn entails that the actions of relational
interpretations of types on objects and on morphisms in
\mathsf{RelEnv}
are
intertwined. As for set interpretations, we know from
[
JP19
]

that, for every
\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F
,
\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}
is actually in
[\mathsf{Rel}^{k},\mathsf{Rel}]
where
k=|\overline{\alpha}|
. We first define the actions of each of these functors
on morphisms between environments, and then argue that they are
well-defined and have the required properties. To do this, we show
that
T_{H}=(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H},T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H},T^{\mathsf{Rel}}_{H})
is a
higher-order

\omega
-cocontinuous environment transformer
, as defined by

{defi}

A
higher-order
\omega
-cocontinuous environment transformer

is a triple
H=(H^{1},H^{2},H^{*})
, where

•

and

•

For all

and all

and
\pi_{2}(H^{*}f\,F\,\overline{R})=H^{2}(\pi_{2}f)\,F^{2}\,\overline{B}

.
Using

, we can define the functorial action of relational
interpretation.

{defi}

Let
f:\rho\to\rho^{\prime}
for relation environments
\rho
and
\rho^{\prime}

(so that
\rho|_{\mathbb{T}}=\rho^{\prime}|_{\mathbb{T}}
). The action

of

on

is given exactly as in

, except that all interpretations
are relational interpretations and all occurrences of

.

If we define
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket
to be
(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}},\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}},\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}})
, then this is an immediate consequence of

Lemma 2
.

For every
\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F
,

is an

\omega
-cocontinuous environment transformer.

The proof is a straightforward induction on the structure of

,
using an appropriate result from
[
JP19
]
to deduce

\omega
-cocontinuity of
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket

together with Lemma

and Equation

for

-types. We note that, for any relation environment
