,

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket\rho\in RT_{0}
.

We can prove by simultaneous induction that our interpretations of
types interact well with demotion of functorial variables to
non-functorial ones, along with other useful identities. Indeed, if

\rho,\rho^{\prime}:\mathsf{SetEnv}
,
f:\rho\to\rho^{\prime}
,
\rho\phi=\rho\psi=\rho^{\prime}\phi=\rho^{\prime}\psi
,
f\phi=f\psi=\mathit{id}_{\rho\phi}
,
\Gamma;\Phi,\phi^{k}\vdash F
,

\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash G
,
\Gamma;\Phi,\alpha_{1}\dots\alpha_{k}\vdash H
, and
\overline{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}
, then

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho=\llbracket{\Gamma,\psi;\Phi\vdash F[\phi:==\psi]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho

(4)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f=\llbracket{\Gamma,\psi;\Phi\vdash F[\phi:==\psi]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f

(5)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}]

(6)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f}]

(7)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F[\phi:=H]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\phi:=\lambda\overline{A}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=A}]]

(8)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F[\phi:=H]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f[\phi:=\lambda\overline{A}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{A}}]]

(9)

Identities analogous to (
4
) through
(
9
) hold for relational interpretations as well.

4.
The Identity Extension Lemma

In most treatments of parametricity, equality relations on sets are
taken as
given
— either directly as diagonal relations, or
perhaps via reflexive graphs if kinds are also being tracked — and
the graph relations used to validate existence of initial algebras are
defined in terms of them. We take a different approach here, giving a
categorical definition of graph relations for morphisms (i.e., natural
transformations) between functors and
constructing
equality
relations as particular graph relations. Our definitions specialize to
the usual ones for the graph relation for morphisms between sets and
equality relations on sets. In light of its novelty, we spell out
our construction in detail.

The standard definition of the graph for a morphism
f:A\to B
in

\mathsf{Set}
is the relation
\langle{f}\rangle:\mathsf{Rel}(A,B)
defined by
(x,y)\in\langle{f}\rangle
iff
fx=y
. This definition naturally generalizes to
associate to each natural transformation between
k
-ary functors on

\mathsf{Set}
a
k
-ary relation transformer as follows:

{defi}

If
F,G:\mathsf{Set}^{k}\to\mathsf{Set}
and
\alpha:F\to G
is a natural
transformation, then the functor
\langle{\alpha}\rangle^{*}:\mathsf{Rel}^{k}\to\mathsf{Rel}

is defined as follows. Given
R_{1}:\mathsf{Rel}(A_{1},B_{1}),\dots,R_{k}:\mathsf{Rel}(A_{k},B_{k})
, let
\iota_{R_{i}}:R_{i}\hookrightarrow A_{i}\times B_{i}
, for
i=1,\dots,k
, be the inclusion of
R_{i}
as a subset of
A_{i}\times B_{i}
,
let
h_{\overline{A\times B}}
be the unique morphism making the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\overline{A\times B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\overline{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{A}\times G\overline{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{\pi_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{\pi_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\overline{A\times B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\overline{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}

commute, and let
h_{\overline{R}}:F\overline{R}\to F\overline{A}\times G\overline{B}
be
h_{\overline{A\times B}}\circ F\overline{\iota_{R}}
. Further, let
\alpha^{\wedge}\overline{R}
be the
subobject through which
h_{\overline{R}}
is factorized by the
mono-epi factorization system in
\mathsf{Set}
, as shown in the
following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{A}\times G\overline{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{\wedge}\overline{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\overline{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{\alpha^{\wedge}\overline{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\alpha^{\wedge}\overline{R}}}

Then
\alpha^{\wedge}\overline{R}:\mathsf{Rel}(F\overline{A},G\overline{B})

by construction, so the action of
\langle\alpha\rangle^{*}
on
objects can be given by
\langle\alpha\rangle^{*}\overline{(A,B,R)}=(F\overline{A},G\overline{B},\iota_{\alpha^{\wedge}\overline{R}}\alpha^{\wedge}\overline{R})
. Its action on morphisms is
given by
\langle{\alpha}\rangle^{*}\overline{(\beta,\beta^{\prime})}=(F\overline{\beta},G\overline{\beta}^{\prime})
.

The data in Definition
4
yield the
graph
relation transformer for
\alpha
, denoted
\langle{\alpha}\rangle=(F,G,\langle{\alpha}\rangle^{*})
.

Lemma 3
.

If
F,G:[\mathsf{Set}^{k},\mathsf{Set}]
, and if
\alpha:F\to G
is a natural
transformation, then
\langle{\alpha}\rangle
is in
RT_{k}
.

Proof 4.1
.

Clearly,
\langle{\alpha}\rangle^{*}
is
\omega
-cocontinuous, so

\langle{\alpha}\rangle^{*}:[\mathsf{Rel}^{k},\mathsf{Rel}]
. Let
\overline{R:\mathsf{Rel}(A,B)}
,
\overline{S:\mathsf{Rel}(C,D)}
, and
\overline{(\beta,\beta^{\prime}):R\to S}
. We want to show that there exists a morphism

\epsilon:\alpha^{\wedge}\overline{R}\to\alpha^{\wedge}\overline{S}

such that the diagram on the left below commutes. Since

\overline{(\beta,\beta^{\prime}):R\to S}
, there exist
\overline{\gamma:R\to S}
such that each diagram in the middle commutes.
Moreover, since both
h_{\overline{C\times D}}\circ F(\overline{\beta\times\beta^{\prime}})
and
(F\overline{\beta}\times G\overline{\beta^{\prime}})\circ h_{\overline{A\times B}}
make the diagram
on the right commute, they must be equal.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{\wedge}\overline{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{A}\times G\overline{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{\wedge}\overline{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{C}\times G\overline{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\alpha^{\wedge}\overline{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{\beta}\times G\overline{\beta^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\alpha^{\wedge}\overline{S}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{i}\times B_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{i}\times D_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{R_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{i}\times\beta^{\prime}_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{S_{i}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{C}\times G\overline{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\overline{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\overline{A\times B})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists!}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{\pi_{1}}\circ F(\overline{\beta\times\beta^{\prime}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\overline{D}}\circ F\overline{\pi_{2}}\circ F(\overline{\beta\times\beta^{\prime}})}

We therefore get that the right-hand square in the diagram on the left
below commutes, and thus that the entire diagram does as well.
Finally, by the left-lifting property of
q_{F^{\wedge}\overline{R}}

with respect to
\iota_{F^{\wedge}\overline{S}}
given by the mono-epi
factorization system, there exists an
\epsilon
such that the diagram
on the right below commutes as desired.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\overline{A\times B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{A}\times G\overline{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\overline{C\times D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{C}\times G\overline{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{\gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{\iota_{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\overline{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\overline{\beta\times\beta^{\prime}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\overline{A\times B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{\beta}\times G\overline{\beta^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{\iota_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\overline{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\overline{C\times D}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{\wedge}\overline{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{A}\times G\overline{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{\wedge}\overline{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{C}\times G\overline{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{\gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{\alpha^{\wedge}\overline{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\alpha^{\wedge}\overline{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{\beta}\times G\overline{\beta^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{\alpha^{\wedge}\overline{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\alpha^{\wedge}\overline{S}}}

∎

If
f:A\to B
is a morphism in
\mathsf{Set}
then the definition of the
graph relation transformer
\langle f\rangle
for
f
as a natural
transformation between
0
-ary functors
A
and
B
coincides with its
standard definition. Graph relation transformers are thus a
reasonable extension of graph relations to functors.

The action of a graph relation transformer on a graph relation can be
computed explicitly:

Lemma 4
.

If
\alpha:F\to G
is a morphism in
[\mathsf{Set}^{k},\mathsf{Set}]

and
f_{1}:A_{1}\to B_{1},\dots,f_{k}:A_{k}\to B_{k}
,
then
\langle{\alpha}\rangle^{*}\langle{\overline{f}}\rangle=\langle G\overline{f}\circ\alpha_{\overline{A}}\rangle=\langle\alpha_{\overline{B}}\circ F\overline{f}\rangle
.

Proof 4.2
.

Since
h_{\overline{A\times B}}
is the unique morphism making the
bottom triangle of the diagram on the left below commute, and since

h_{\langle{\overline{f}}\rangle}=h_{\overline{A\times B}}\circ F\,\overline{\iota_{\langle{f}\rangle}}=h_{\overline{A\times B}}\circ F\overline{\langle\mathit{id}_{A},f\rangle}
, the universal property of the
product depicted in the diagram on the right gives

h_{\langle{\overline{f}}\rangle}=\langle\mathit{id}_{F\overline{A}},\alpha_{\overline{B}}\circ F\overline{f}\rangle:F\overline{A}\to F\overline{A}\times G\overline{B}
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\overline{A\times B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\overline{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{A}\times G\overline{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{\langle\mathit{id}_{A},f\rangle}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{\pi_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{\pi_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{\overline{A\times B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\overline{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{A}\times G\overline{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\overline{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F{\overline{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists!}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\overline{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\overline{B}}}

Moreover,
\langle\mathit{id}_{F\overline{A}},\alpha_{\overline{B}}\circ F\overline{f}\rangle
is a monomorphism in
\mathsf{Set}
because
\mathit{id}_{F\overline{A}}
is, so
its mono-epi factorization gives
\iota_{\alpha^{\wedge}\langle{\overline{f}}\rangle}=\langle\mathit{id}_{F\overline{A}},\alpha_{\overline{B}}\circ F\overline{f}\rangle
, and thus
\alpha^{\wedge}\langle{\overline{f}}\rangle=F\overline{A}
. Then
\iota_{\alpha^{\wedge}\langle{\overline{f}}\rangle}\alpha^{\wedge}\langle{\overline{f}}\rangle=\langle\mathit{id}_{F\overline{A}},\alpha_{\overline{B}}\circ F\overline{f}\rangle(F\overline{A})=\langle{\alpha_{\overline{B}}\circ F\overline{f}}\rangle^{*}
, so that

\langle{\alpha}\rangle^{*}\langle{\overline{f}}\rangle=(F\overline{A},G\overline{B},\iota_{\alpha^{\wedge}\langle{\overline{f}}\rangle}\,\alpha^{\wedge}\langle{\overline{f}}\rangle)=(F\overline{A},G\overline{B},\langle{\alpha_{\overline{B}}\circ F\overline{f}}\rangle^{*})=\langle{\alpha_{\overline{B}}\circ F\overline{f}}\rangle

Finally,
\alpha_{\overline{B}}\circ F\overline{f}=G\overline{f}\circ\alpha_{\overline{A}}
by naturality of
\alpha
.
∎

To prove the IEL, we also need to know that the equality relation
transformer preserves equality relations. The
equality relation
transformer
on
F:[\mathsf{Set}^{k},\mathsf{Set}]
is defined to be

\mathsf{Eq}_{F}=\langle{\mathit{id}_{F}}\rangle=(F,F,\langle{\mathit{id}_{F}}\rangle^{*})

(10)

Lemma
4
then gives that, for all
\overline{A:\mathsf{Set}}
,

\mathsf{Eq}^{*}_{F}\overline{\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}=\langle{\mathit{id}_{F}}\rangle^{*}\langle{\mathit{id}_{\overline{A}}}\rangle=\langle{F\mathit{id}_{\overline{A}}\circ(\mathit{id}_{F})_{\overline{A}}}\rangle=\langle{\mathit{id}_{F\overline{A}}\circ\mathit{id}_{F\overline{A}}}\rangle=\langle{\mathit{id}_{F\overline{A}}}\rangle=\mathsf{Eq}_{F\overline{A}}

(11)

Graph relation transformers in general, and equality relation
transformers in particular, naturally extend to relation environments.
Indeed, if
\rho,\rho^{\prime}:\mathsf{SetEnv}
and
f:\rho\to\rho^{\prime}
, then the

graph relation environment

\langle{f}\rangle
is defined pointwise by

\langle{f}\rangle\phi=\langle{f\phi}\rangle
for every
\phi
, which entails that

\pi_{1}\langle{f}\rangle=\rho
and
\pi_{2}\langle{f}\rangle=\rho^{\prime}
. In particular,
the
equality relation environment

\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}
is defined to be

\langle{\mathit{id}_{\rho}}\rangle
, which entails that
\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}\phi=\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho\phi}
for every
\phi
.

With these definitions in hand, we can state and prove both an
Identity Extension Lemma and a Graph Lemma for our calculus.

Theorem 5

(IEL)
.

If
\rho:\mathsf{SetEnv}
and
\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F
then
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}=\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}
.

Proof 4.3
.

The proof is by induction on the structure of
F
. Only the
\mathsf{Nat}
,
application, and fixpoint cases are non-routine.

•

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\mathbb{0}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}=0_{\mathsf{Rel}}=\mathsf{Eq}_{0_{\mathsf{Set}}}=\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\mathbb{0}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}

•

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\mathbb{1}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}=1_{\mathsf{Rel}}=\mathsf{Eq}_{1_{\mathsf{Set}}}=\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\mathbb{1}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}

•

By definition,
\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}
is the relation on

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
relating
t
and
t^{\prime}
if, for all
{R_{1}:\mathsf{Rel}(A_{1},B_{1})},\dots,{R_{k}:\mathsf{Rel}(A_{k},B_{k})}
,
(t_{\overline{A}},t^{\prime}_{\overline{B}})
is a morphism from
\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}\overline{[\alpha:=R]}
to

\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}\overline{[\alpha:=R]}
in
\mathsf{Rel}
. To prove that this
relation is
\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}
we must show that

(t_{\overline{A}},t^{\prime}_{\overline{B}})
is a morphism from

\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}\overline{[\alpha:=R]}
to
\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}\overline{[\alpha:=R]}
in

\mathsf{Rel}
for all
{R_{1}:\mathsf{Rel}(A_{1},B_{1})},\dots,{R_{k}:\mathsf{Rel}(A_{k},B_{k})}
if
and only if
t=t^{\prime}
. We first show that if
(t_{\overline{A}},t^{\prime}_{\overline{B}})
is a morphism from
\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}\overline{[\alpha:=R]}
to

\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}\overline{[\alpha:=R]}
for all
{R_{1}:\mathsf{Rel}(A_{1},B_{1}),}

\dots,{R_{k}:\mathsf{Rel}(A_{k},B_{k})}
then
t=t^{\prime}
. By hypothesis, for all

A_{1}\,\dots\,A_{k}:\mathsf{Set}
,
(t_{\overline{A}},t^{\prime}_{\overline{A}})
is a
morphism from
\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}\overline{[\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}]}
to
\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}\overline{[\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}]}
. By the induction hypothesis, it is therefore a
morphism from
\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\overline{[\alpha:=A]}}
to
\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\overline{[\alpha:=A]}}
in

\mathsf{Rel}
. This means that, for all
x:\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\overline{[\alpha:=A]}}
,

t_{\overline{A}}x=t^{\prime}_{\overline{A}}x
, so
t=t^{\prime}
by
extensionality. For the converse, we observe that if
t=t^{\prime}
then
the desired conclusion is simply the extra condition in the
definition of
\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
.

•

The application case is proved by the following sequence of
equalities, where the second equality is by the induction hypothesis
and the definition of the relation environment
\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}
, the third
is by the definition of application of relation transformers from
Definition
3.2
, and the fourth is by
Equation
11
:

\begin{split}\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\phi\overline{F}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}&=(\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}\phi)\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}\\
&=\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho\phi}\,\overline{\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}}\\
&=(\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho\phi})^{*}\,\overline{\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}}\\
&=\mathsf{Eq}_{(\rho\phi)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}}\\
&=\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\phi\overline{F}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}\end{split}

•

The fixpoint case is proven by the sequence of equalities

\begin{split}\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{F}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}&=(\mu{T_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}})\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}\\
&={{\underrightarrow{\lim}}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}{T^{n}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}}}\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}\\
&={{\underrightarrow{\lim}}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}{T^{n}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}\,\overline{\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}}}}\\
&={{\underrightarrow{\lim}}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}{(\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n}K_{0}})^{*}\overline{\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}}}}\\
&={{\underrightarrow{\lim}}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n}K_{0}\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}}}}\\
&=\mathsf{Eq}_{{{\underrightarrow{\lim}}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n}K_{0}\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}}}}\\
&=\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{F}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}\end{split}

Here, the third equality is by induction hypothesis, the fifth is by
Equation
11
, and the fourth equality is because, for
every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the following two statements can be proved by
simultaneous induction: for any
H
,
\rho
, and
A
,

T^{n}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}\,\overline{\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}=(\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n}K_{0}})^{*}\overline{\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}

(12)

and for any subformula
J
of
H
,

\begin{split}&\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash J}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=T^{n}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}]\overline{[\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}]}\\
=\;\;&\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash J}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n}K_{0}}]\overline{[\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}]}\end{split}

(13)

The case
n=0
is trivial:
Equation
12
and
Equation
13
both hold because

T^{0}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}=K_{0}
,
(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{0}K_{0}=K_{0}
, and
Equation
11
holds.

The inductive case is proved as follows. We prove
Equation
12
by the following
sequence of equalities:

\begin{split}T^{n+1}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}\,\overline{\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}&=T^{\mathsf{Rel}}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}(T^{n}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0})\overline{\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}\\
&=\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=T^{n}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}]\overline{[\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}]}\\
&=\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n}K_{0}}]\overline{[\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}]}\\
&=\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho[\phi:=(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n}K_{0}]\overline{[\alpha:=A]}}\\
&=\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\phi:=(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n}K_{0}]\overline{[\alpha:=A]}}\\
&=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}\overline{A}}\\
&=(\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}})^{*}\,\overline{\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}\end{split}

Here, the third equality is by the induction hypothesis of
Equation
13
for
J=H
, the fifth
by the induction hypothesis of the IEL on
H
, and the last is by
Equation
11
.

We prove Equation
13
by structural
induction on
J
. The only interesting cases, though, are when
J=\phi\overline{G}
and when
J=(\mu\psi.\lambda\overline{\beta}.G)\overline{K}
.

–

The case
J=\phi\overline{G}
is proved by the sequence of equalities:

\begin{split}&\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash\phi\overline{G}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=T^{n+1}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}]\overline{[\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}]}\\
&=T^{n+1}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=T^{n+1}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}]\overline{[\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}]}}\\
&=T^{n+1}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}}]\overline{[\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}]}}\\
&=T^{n+1}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho[\phi:=(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}]\overline{[\alpha:=A]}}}\\
&=T^{n+1}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}\,\overline{\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\,\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\phi:=(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}]\overline{[\alpha:=A]}}}\\
&=(\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}})^{*}\,\overline{\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\,\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\phi:=(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}]\overline{[\alpha:=A]}}}\\
&=(\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}})^{*}\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}}]\overline{[\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}]}}\\
&=\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash\phi\overline{G}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}}]\overline{[\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}]}\end{split}

Here, the second equality is by the induction hypothesis for
Equation
13
on the
G
s, the fourth
is by the induction hypothesis for the IEL on the
G
s, and the fifth
is by Equation
12
, which we have
just proved.

–

The case
J=(\mu\psi.\,\lambda\overline{\beta}.\,G)\overline{K}

is proved by the sequence of equalities

\begin{split}&\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash(\mu\psi.\,\lambda\overline{\beta}.\,G)\overline{K}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=T^{n+1}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]\\
&=(\mu{T_{G,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=T^{n+1}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}})\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=T^{n+1}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}\\
&={{\underrightarrow{\lim}}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}{T^{m}_{G,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=T^{n+1}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}}}\,K_{0}\,(\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=T^{n+1}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]})\\
&={{\underrightarrow{\lim}}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}{T^{m}_{G,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=T^{n+1}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}}}\,K_{0}\,(\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]})\\
&={{\underrightarrow{\lim}}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}{T^{m}_{G,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}}}\,K_{0}\,(\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]})\\
&=(\mu{T_{G,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}})\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}\\
&=\llbracket{\Gamma;\phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash(\mu\psi.\,\lambda\beta.\,G)\overline{K}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]\end{split}

Here, the third equality is by the induction hypothesis for
Equation
13
on the
K
s, and the
fourth equality holds because we can prove that, for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

T^{m}_{G,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=T^{n+1}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}\,K_{0}\ =T^{m}_{G,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}\,K_{0}

(14)

Indeed, the base case of Equation
14
is trivial because

T^{0}_{G,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=T^{n+1}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}\,K_{0}\ =K_{0}=T^{0}_{G,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}\,K_{0}

and the inductive case is proved by:

\displaystyle T^{m+1}_{G,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=T^{n+1}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}\,K_{0}

\displaystyle=T_{G,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=T^{n+1}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}(T^{m}_{G,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=T^{n+1}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}\,K_{0})

\displaystyle=T_{G,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=T^{n+1}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}(T^{m}_{G,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}\,K_{0})

\displaystyle=\lambda\overline{R}.\llbracket{\Gamma;\psi,\overline{\beta}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=T^{n+1}_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}}K_{0}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}][\psi:=T^{m}_{G,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}\,K_{0}][\overline{\beta:=R}]

\displaystyle=\lambda\overline{R}.\llbracket{\Gamma;\psi,\overline{\beta}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}][\psi:=T^{m}_{G,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}\,K_{0}][\overline{\beta:=R}]

\displaystyle=T_{G,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}(T^{m}_{G,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}\,K_{0})

\displaystyle=T^{m+1}_{G,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\phi:=\mathsf{Eq}_{(T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})^{n+1}K_{0}}][\overline{\alpha:=\mathsf{Eq}_{A}}]}\,K_{0}

Here, the second equality holds by the induction hypothesis for
Equation
14
on
m
. The fourth equality holds because

\phi
and the variables in
\overline{\alpha}
do not appear in
G
. (We note
that this case of the IEL would fail to hold if the functorial context
in which the body
F
of a
\mu
-type
\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.F
were formed in Definition
2.1
were
extended to contain variables other than
\phi
and the
\alpha
s.
This justifies our design of the
\mu
-type formation rule there.)

This concludes the fixpoint case.

•

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F+G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}+\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}=\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}+\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}=\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho+\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}=\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F+G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}

•

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F\times G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}\times\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}=\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}\times\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}=\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\times\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}=\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F\times G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}

With the IEL in hand we can prove a Graph Lemma appropriate to our
setting:

Lemma 6

(Graph Lemma)
.

If
\rho,\rho^{\prime}:\mathsf{SetEnv}
and
f:\rho\to\rho^{\prime}
then

\langle{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f}\rangle=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\langle{f}\rangle
.

Proof 4.4
.

Applying Lemma
2
to the morphisms
(f,\mathit{id}_{\rho^{\prime}}):\langle{f}\rangle\to\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho^{\prime}}
and
(\mathit{id}_{\rho},f):\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}\to\langle{f}\rangle
of relation environments gives

\begin{array}[]{ll}&(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\mathit{id}_{\rho^{\prime}})\\
=&\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}(f,\mathit{id}_{\rho^{\prime}}):\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\langle{f}\rangle\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho^{\prime}}\end{array}

and

\begin{array}[]{ll}&(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\mathit{id}_{\rho},\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f)\\
=&\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}(\mathit{id}_{\rho},f):\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\langle{f}\rangle\end{array}

Expanding the first equation gives that if
(x,y)\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\langle{f}\rangle
then

(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f\,x,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\mathit{id}_{\rho^{\prime}}\,y)\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho^{\prime}}

So
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\mathit{id}_{\rho^{\prime}}\,y=\mathit{id}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho^{\prime}}\,y=y
and
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho^{\prime}}=\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho^{\prime}}
, and if
(x,y)\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\langle{f}\rangle
then
(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f\,x,y)\in\mathsf{Eq}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho^{\prime}}
,
i.e.,
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f\,x=y
, i.e.,
(x,y)\in\langle{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f}\rangle
. So, we have

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\langle{f}\rangle\subseteq\langle{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f}\rangle
.

Expanding the second equation gives that if
x\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
then

(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\mathit{id}_{\rho}\,x,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f\,x)\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\langle{f}\rangle

Then
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\mathit{id}_{\rho}\,x=\mathit{id}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}x=x
, so for any
x\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
we have
(x,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f\,x)

\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\langle{f}\rangle
.
Moreover,
x\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
if and only if

(x,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f\,x)\in\langle{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f}\rangle

and, if
x\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
then
(x,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f\,x)\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\langle{f}\rangle
. Thus, if

(x,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f\,x)\in\langle{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f}\rangle

then
(x,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f\,x)\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\langle{f}\rangle
, i.e.,
\langle{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f}\rangle\subseteq\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\langle{f}\rangle
. ∎

5.
Interpreting Terms

If
\Delta=x_{1}:F_{1},\dots,x_{n}:F_{n}
is a term context for
\Gamma

and
\Phi
, then the interpretations
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
and
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}
are defined by

\begin{array}[]{lll}\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}&=&\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F_{1}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\times\cdots\times\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F_{n}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\\
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}&=&\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F_{1}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\times\cdots\times\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F_{n}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\\
\end{array}

Every well-formed term
\Gamma;\Phi~|~\Delta\vdash t:F
then has,
for every
\rho\in\mathsf{SetEnv}
, a set interpretation

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi~|~\Delta\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
as the component at

\rho
of a natural transformation from
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
to
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
, and, for every
\rho\in\mathsf{RelEnv}
, a relational interpretation

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi~|~\Delta\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho
as the component at

\rho
of a natural transformation from
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}
to
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}
.

{defi}

If
\rho
is a set (resp., relation) environment and

\Gamma;\Phi~|~\Delta\vdash t:F
then

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi~|~\Delta\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
(resp.,

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi~|~\Delta\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho
) is defined as in
Figure
2
, where
\mathsf{D}
is either
\mathsf{Set}
or

\mathsf{Rel}
as appropriate.

If
t
is closed, i.e., if
\emptyset;\emptyset~|~\emptyset\vdash t:F
, then we write
\llbracket{\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}
instead of
\llbracket{\emptyset;\emptyset~|~\emptyset\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}
. The interpretations in
Definition
5
respect weakening, i.e., a term and
its weakenings all have the same set and relational
interpretations. Specifically, for any
\rho\in\mathsf{SetEnv}
,

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta,x:F\vdash t:G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho=(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho)\circ\pi_{\Delta}

where
\pi_{\Delta}
is the projection

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta,x:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
. A similar result holds for
relational interpretations.

\begin{array}[]{lll}\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta,x:F\vdash x:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho&=&\pi_{|\Delta|+1}\\
\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash L_{\overline{\alpha}}x.t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho&=&\mathsf{curry}(\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\,|\,\Delta,x:F\vdash t:G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=\_}])\\
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash t_{\overline{K}}s:G[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho&=&\mathsf{eval}\circ\langle\lambda d.\,(\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho\;d)_{\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho}},\\
&&\hskip 36.135pt\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash s:F[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho\rangle\\
&&\\
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash\bot_{F}t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho&=&!^{0}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho}\circ\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi~|~\Delta\vdash t:\mathbb{0}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho,\mbox{ where }\\
&&\hskip 7.22743pt!^{0}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho}\mbox{ is the unique morphism from }0\\
&&\hskip 7.22743pt\mbox{ to }\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho\\
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash\top:\mathbb{1}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho&=&!^{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho}_{1},\mbox{ where }!^{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho}_{1}\\
&&\hskip 7.22743pt\mbox{ is the unique morphism from }\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho\mbox{ to }1\\
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash(s,t):F\times G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho&=&\langle\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash s:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho,\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho\rangle\\
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash\pi_{1}t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho&=&\pi_{1}\circ\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:F\times G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho\\
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash\pi_{2}t:G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho&=&\pi_{2}\circ\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:F\times G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho\\
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi~|~\Delta\vdash\mathsf{case}\,{t}\,\mathsf{of}\,\{{x\mapsto l};\,{y\mapsto r}\}:K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho&=&\mathsf{eval}\circ\langle\mathsf{curry}\,[\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta,x:F\vdash l:K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho,\\
&&\hskip 57.0928pt\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta,y:G\vdash r:K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho],\\
&&\hskip 36.135pt\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:F+G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho\rangle\\
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash\mathsf{inl}\,s:F+G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho&=&\mathsf{inl}\circ\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash s:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho\\
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash\mathsf{inr}\,t:F+G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho&=&\mathsf{inr}\circ\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho\\
\llbracket\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{map}^{\overline{F},\overline{G}}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}(\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta},\overline{\gamma}}F\,G)&=&\lambda d\,\overline{\eta}\,\overline{C}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\phi},\overline{\gamma}\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\mathit{id}_{\rho[\overline{\gamma:=C}]}[\overline{\phi:=\lambda\overline{B}.\eta_{\overline{B}\,\overline{C}}}]\\
\hskip 36.135pt(\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\gamma}}\,H[\overline{\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}F}]\,H[\overline{\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}G}])\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho&&\\
\llbracket\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{in}_{H}:Nat^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]&=&\lambda d.\,\mathit{in}_{{T}^{X}_{H,\rho}}\;\;\mbox{ where }X\mbox{ is }\mathsf{Set}\mbox{ when }\\
\hskip 57.0928pt(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho&&\hskip 14.45377pt\mathsf{D}=\mathsf{Set}\mbox{ and not present when }\mathsf{D}=\mathsf{Rel}\\
\llbracket\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{fold}^{F}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}F][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\,F)&=&\lambda d.\,\mathit{fold}_{T^{X}_{H,\rho}}\\
\hskip 57.0928pt(\mathsf{Nat}^{{\overline{\beta}}}\,(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}\;F)\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho&&\hskip 14.45377pt\mbox{ where }X\mbox{ is as above
}\end{array}

Figure 2
.
Term semantics

The return type for the semantic fold is

\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho[\overline{\beta:=B}]
.
This interpretation gives

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash L_{x}.t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho=\mathsf{curry}(\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta,x:F\vdash t:G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho)

and

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash st:G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho=\mathsf{eval}\circ\langle\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash s:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho,\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho\rangle

so it specializes to the standard interpretations of those System F
terms that are representable in our calculus. Term interpretation
also respects substitution for both functorial and non-functorial type
variables, as well as term substitution. That is, if

\Gamma,\alpha;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:F
and
\Gamma;\Phi,\alpha\,|\,\Delta\vdash t^{\prime}:F
and
\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G
then

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta[\alpha:=G]\vdash t[\alpha:=G]:F[\alpha:=G]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho=\llbracket{\Gamma,\alpha;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho[\alpha:=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho]

and

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta[\alpha:=G]\vdash t^{\prime}[\alpha:=G]:F[\alpha:=G]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\alpha\,|\,\Delta\vdash t^{\prime}:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho[\alpha:=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho]

and if
\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta,x:G\vdash t:F
and
\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash s:G
then

\lambda A.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash t[x:=s]:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho\,A=\lambda A.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta,x:G\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho\,(A,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash s:G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\rho\,A)

Direct calculation further reveals that term interpretations also
satisfy

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi~|~\Delta\vdash(L_{\overline{\alpha}}x.t)_{\overline{K}}s}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi~|~\Delta\vdash t[\overline{\alpha:=K}][x:=s]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}

and

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\Delta\vdash L_{\overline{\alpha}}x.t_{\overline{\alpha}}x:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}=\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\Delta\vdash t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}

6.
Naturality and the Abstraction Theorem

6.1.
Naturality and Its Consequences

We first show that terms of
\mathsf{Nat}
-type behave as natural
transformations with respect to their source and target functorial
types, and derive some conseqences of this observation. If
\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F
then define the
identity

\mathit{id}_{F}
on
F
by

\mathit{id}_{F}=\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\emptyset\vdash L_{\overline{\alpha}}x.x:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}F\,F
. Also, recall from Section
2.2
that if

\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}F\,G
and
\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash s:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}G\,H
are terms then the composition
s\circ t
of
t
and
s
is defined by
s\circ t=\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash L_{\overline{\alpha}}x.s_{\overline{\alpha}}(t_{\overline{\alpha}}x):\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}F\,H
. Then

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\emptyset\vdash\mathit{id}_{F}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}F\,F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\,\ast=\mathit{id}_{\lambda\overline{A}.\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=A}]}

for any set environment
\rho
, and

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash s\circ t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}F\,H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}=\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash s:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}G\,H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\circ\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

Naturality of term interpretations is then easily verified:

Theorem 7
.

If
\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash s:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha},\overline{\gamma}}F\,G
and
\overline{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\gamma}}K\,H}
, then

\begin{array}[]{l}\hskip 9.75604pt\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash((\mathsf{map}_{G}^{\overline{K},\overline{H}})_{\emptyset}\,\overline{t})\circ(L_{\overline{\gamma}}z.s_{\overline{K},\overline{\gamma}}z):\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\gamma}}F[\overline{\alpha:=K}]\,G[\overline{\alpha:=H}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\\
=\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash(L_{\overline{\gamma}}z.s_{\overline{H},\overline{\gamma}}z)\circ((\mathsf{map}_{F}^{\overline{K},\overline{H}})_{\emptyset}\,\overline{t}):\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\gamma}}F[\overline{\alpha:=K}]\,G[\overline{\alpha:=H}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\end{array}

Theorem
7
gives rise to an entire family of free
theorems that are consequences of naturality, and thus do not require
the full power of parametricity. In particular,
Definition
5
alone ensures that standard properties
of the initial algebraic constructs
\mathsf{map}
,
\mathsf{in}
, and
\mathsf{fold}

hold. We have, for example, that the interpretation of every
\mathsf{map}_{H}

is a functor, i.e., if
\Gamma;\overline{\alpha},\overline{\gamma}\vdash H
,

\overline{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash g:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\gamma}}F\,G}
, and
\overline{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash f:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\gamma}}G\,K}
, then

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash(\mathsf{map}^{\overline{F},\overline{K}}_{H}\,)_{\emptyset}\,\overline{(f\circ g)}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\gamma}}H[\overline{\alpha:=F}]\,H[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

\displaystyle=\hskip 2.168pt

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash(\mathsf{map}^{\overline{G},\overline{K}}_{H}\,)_{\emptyset}\,\overline{f}\circ(\mathsf{map}^{\overline{F},\overline{G}}_{H}\,)_{\emptyset}\,\overline{g}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\gamma}}H[\overline{\alpha:=F}]\,H[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

In fact, we have that if
\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash H
,
\overline{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash F}
, and
\overline{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash G}
, then,
for all
\overline{f\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}
,

\begin{array}[]{rl}&\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\overline{x:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}F\,G}\vdash(\mathsf{map}^{\overline{F},\overline{G}}_{H})_{\emptyset}\,\overline{x}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\,H[\overline{\alpha:=F}]\,H[\overline{\alpha:=G}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\,\overline{f}\\
=&\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\mathit{id}_{\rho}[\overline{\alpha:=f}]\\
=&\mathit{map}_{\lambda\overline{A}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=A}]}\overline{f}\\
\end{array}

Here, we obtain the first equality from the appropriate instance of
Definition
5
, and the second one by noting that

\lambda\overline{A}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=A}]
is a functor in
\overline{A}
and using

\mathit{map}_{G}\,\overline{f}
to denote the action of the semantic functor

G
on morphisms
\overline{f}
. We also have, e.g., that:

\bullet

\mathsf{map}
is a higher-order functor, i.e., if
\Gamma;\overline{\psi},\overline{\gamma}\vdash H
,
\overline{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha},\overline{\gamma},\overline{\phi}\vdash K}
,
\overline{\Gamma;\overline{\beta},\overline{\gamma}\vdash F}
,
\overline{\Gamma;\overline{\beta},\overline{\gamma}\vdash G}
, and
\xi=\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}(\overline{\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha},\overline{\beta},\overline{\gamma}}F\,G})\,(\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\gamma}}H[\overline{\psi:=K}][\overline{\phi:=F}]\,H[\overline{\psi:=K}][\overline{\phi:=G}])
, then

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{map}_{H[\overline{\psi:=K}]}^{\overline{F},\overline{G}}:\xi}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}=\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\emptyset\vdash L_{\emptyset}\overline{z}.(\mathsf{map}_{H}^{\overline{K[\overline{\phi:=F}]},\overline{K[\overline{\phi:=G}]}})_{\emptyset}\,(\overline{(\mathsf{map}_{K}^{\overline{F},\overline{G}})_{\emptyset}\,\overline{z}}):\xi}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

\bullet

\mathsf{fold}
behaves as expected, i.e., if
\xi=\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\;F
, then

\hskip-72.26999pt\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,x:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}H[\phi:=F][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\,F\vdash((\mathsf{fold}_{H,F})_{\emptyset}x)\circ\mathsf{in}_{H}:\xi}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

=\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,x:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}H[\phi:=F][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\,F\vdash x\circ\big((\mathsf{map}_{H[\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]}^{(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}H)\overline{\beta},F})_{\emptyset}((\mathsf{fold}_{H,F})_{\emptyset}x)\big):\xi}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

\bullet

\mathsf{in}
has a right inverse, i.e., if
\xi=\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}\;(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}
, then

\hskip 31.07556pt\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{in}_{H}\circ(\mathsf{fold}_{H,H[\phi:=(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}]})_{\emptyset}((\mathsf{map}_{H}^{H[\phi:=(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}],(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}})_{\emptyset}\mathsf{in}_{H}):\xi}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

\hskip-250.05467pt=\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\emptyset\vdash\mathit{id}_{(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}}:\xi}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

\bullet

\mathsf{in}
has a left inverse, i.e., if
\xi=\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}H[\phi:=(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}]\,H[\phi:=(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}]
, then

\hskip 29.63095pt\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\emptyset\vdash(\mathsf{fold}_{H,H[\phi:=(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}]})_{\emptyset}((\mathsf{map}_{H}^{H[\phi:=(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}],(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}})_{\emptyset}\mathsf{in}_{H})\circ\mathsf{in}_{H}:\xi}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

\hskip-227.65005pt=\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\emptyset\vdash\mathit{id}_{H[\phi:=(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}]}:\xi}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

Analogous results hold for relational interpretations of terms and
relation environments.

6.2.
The Abstraction Theorem

To get consequences of parametricity that are not merely consequences
of naturality, we prove an Abstraction Theorem
(Theorem
10
) for our calculus. As is usual for such
theorems, we first prove a more general result
(Theorem
8
) for open terms, and recover our Abstraction
Theorem as a special case of it for closed terms of closed type.

Theorem 8
.

Every well-formed term
\Gamma;\Phi~|~\Delta\vdash t:F
induces
a morphism from
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket
to

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket
, i.e., a triple of natural
transformations

(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi~|~\Delta\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}},\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi~|~\Delta\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}},\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi~|~\Delta\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}})

where, for
\mathsf{D}\in\{\mathsf{Set},\mathsf{Rel}\}
and for
\rho\in\mathsf{SetEnv}

or
\rho\in\mathsf{RelEnv}
as appropriate,

\begin{array}[]{lll}\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi~|~\Delta\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}&:&\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{D}}\end{array}

has as its component at
\rho:\mathsf{SetEnv}
a morphism

\begin{array}[]{lll}\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi~|~\Delta\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho&:&\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\end{array}

Moreover, for all
\rho:\mathsf{RelEnv}
,

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi~|~\Delta\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho=(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi~|~\Delta\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{1}\rho),\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi~|~\Delta\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{2}\rho))

(15)

Proof 6.1
.

The proof is by induction on
t
. It requires showing that set
and relational interpretations of term judgments are natural
transformations, and that all set interpretations of terms of
\mathsf{Nat}

types satisfy the appropriate equality preservation conditions from
Definition
3.1
. For the interesting cases of
abstraction, application,
\mathsf{map}
,
\mathsf{in}
, and
\mathsf{fold}
terms,
propagating the naturality conditions is quite involved; the latter
two especially require some rather delicate diagram chasing. That it
is possible provides strong evidence that our development is sensible,
natural, and at an appropriate level of abstraction.

The only interesting cases are the cases for abstraction, application,

\mathsf{map}
,
\mathsf{in}
, and
\mathsf{fold}
. We omit the others.

•

\underline{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash L_{\overline{\alpha}}x.t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}
To
see that
\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash L_{\overline{\alpha}}x.t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
is
a natural transformation from
\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
to
\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
we need to show that, for every

\rho:\mathsf{SetEnv}
,
\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash L_{\overline{\alpha}}x.t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho

is a morphism in
\mathsf{Set}
from
\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
to
\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
, and that such a family of
morphisms is natural. First, we need to show that, for all
\overline{A:\mathsf{Set}}
and all
d:\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho=\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=A}]
, we have that

(\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash L_{\overline{\alpha}}x.t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\,d)_{\overline{A}}:\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=A}]\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=A}]

but this follows easily from the induction hypothesis.
That these maps comprise a natural transformation
\eta:\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=\_}]\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=\_}]
is
clear since

\eta_{\overline{A}}\,=\,\mathsf{curry}\,(\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\,|\,\Delta,x:F\vdash t:G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=A}])\,d

is the component at
\overline{A}
of
the partial specialization to
d
of the natural transformation

\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\,|\,\Delta,x:F\vdash t:G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=\_}]

To see that the components of

\eta
also satisfy the additional condition needed for
\eta
to
be in
\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
, let
\overline{R:\mathsf{Rel}(A,B)}
and suppose

\begin{array}[]{lll}(u,v)&\in&\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\\
&=&(\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=A}],\\
&&\,\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=B}],(\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\overline{\alpha:=R}])^{*})\end{array}

Then the induction hypothesis and

(d,d)\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}=\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\overline{\alpha:=R}]

ensure that

\begin{array}[]{ll}&(\eta_{\overline{A}}u,\eta_{\overline{B}}v)\\
=&(\mathsf{curry}\,(\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\,|\,\Delta,x:F\vdash t:G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=A}])\,d\,u,\\
&\hskip 36.135pt\mathsf{curry}\,(\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\,|\,\Delta,x:F\vdash t:G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=B}])\,d\,v)\\
=&\mathsf{curry}\,(\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\,|\,\Delta,x:F\vdash t:G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\overline{\alpha:=R}])\,(d,d)\,(u,v)\\
:&\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\end{array}

Moreover,

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash L_{\overline{\alpha}}x.t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho

is trivially natural in
\rho
,
as the functorial action of

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

and
\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

on morphisms is the identity.

•

\underline{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash t_{\overline{K}}s:G[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}

To see that
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash t_{\overline{K}}s:G[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
is a
natural transformation from
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
to

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
we
show that, for every
\rho:\mathsf{SetEnv}
,

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash t_{\overline{K}}s:G[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho

is a morphism from
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
to

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
, and
that this family of morphisms is natural in
\rho
. Let
d:\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
. Then

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash t_{\overline{K}}s:G[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,\rho\,d\\
=&(\mathsf{eval}\circ\langle(\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\;\_)_{\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}},\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash s:F[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\rangle)\,d\\
=&\mathsf{eval}((\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\;\_)_{\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}}\,d,\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash s:F[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\,d)\\
=&\mathsf{eval}((\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\;d)_{\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}},\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash s:F[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\,d)\\
\end{array}

The induction hypothesis ensures that

(\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\,d)_{\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}}

has type

\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}]\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}]
. Since, in addition,

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash s:F[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\,d:\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\\
=&\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}]\\
=&\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}]\end{array}

we have that

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash t_{\overline{K}}s:G[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,\rho\,d:\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}]\\
=&\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\end{array}

as desired.

To see that the family of maps comprising
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash t_{\overline{K}}s:G[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

is natural in
\rho

we need to show that, if
f:\rho\to\rho^{\prime}
in
\mathsf{SetEnv}
, then the
following diagram commutes, where
g=\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
and
h=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash s:F[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\times\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho^{\prime}\times\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle g\rho,h\rho\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle g\rho^{\prime},h\rho^{\prime}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{eval}\circ((-)_{\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket\rho}}\times\mathit{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f\,\times\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{eval}\circ((-)_{\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket\rho^{\prime}}}\times\mathit{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f}

The top diagram commutes because
g
and
h
are natural in
\rho
by
the induction hypothesis.
To see that the bottom diagram commutes,
we need to show that

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f(\eta_{\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket\rho}}x)\\
=&(\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f\,\eta)_{\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket\rho^{\prime}}}(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}fx)\end{array}

holds for all
\eta\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
and
x\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F[\overline{\alpha:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
,
i.e.,
that

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f}]\circ\eta_{\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}}\\
=&\eta_{\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho^{\prime}}}\circ\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f}]\end{array}

for all
\eta\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
.
But this follows from the naturality of
\eta
, which indeed ensures
the commutativity of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho^{\prime}[\overline{\alpha:=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho^{\prime}}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho^{\prime}[\overline{\alpha:=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho^{\prime}}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\;\;\;\eta_{\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}}\;\;\;}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho^{\prime}}}}

•

\underline{\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{map}^{\overline{F},\overline{G}}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\;(\overline{\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta},\overline{\gamma}}\,F\,G})\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\gamma}}\,H[\overline{\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}F}]\,H[\overline{\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}G}])}

To see that

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{map}^{\overline{F},\overline{G}}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\;(\overline{\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta},\overline{\gamma}}\,F\,G})\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\gamma}}\,H[\overline{\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}F}]\,H[\overline{\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}G}])}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,\rho\,d\,\overline{\eta}

is in
\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\gamma}}\,H[\overline{\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}F}]\,H[\overline{\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}G}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho

for all
\rho:\mathsf{SetEnv}
,
d:\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\emptyset}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
,
and

\overline{\eta:\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta},\overline{\gamma}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}

we first note that

\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\phi},\overline{\gamma}\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
is a functor from

\mathsf{SetEnv}
to
\mathsf{Set}
and, for any
\overline{C}
,

\mathit{id}_{\rho[\overline{\gamma:=C}]}[\overline{\phi:=\lambda\overline{B}.\eta_{\overline{B}\,\overline{C}}}]

is a
morphism in
\mathsf{SetEnv}
from

\rho[\overline{\gamma:=C}][\overline{\phi:=\lambda\overline{B}.\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\gamma},\overline{\beta}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\gamma:=C}][\overline{\beta:=B}]}]

to

\rho[\overline{\gamma:=C}][\overline{\phi:=\lambda\overline{B}.\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\gamma},\overline{\beta}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\gamma:=C}][\overline{\beta:=B}]}]

so that

\displaystyle(\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{map}^{\overline{F},\overline{G}}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\;(\overline{\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta},\overline{\gamma}}\,F\,G})\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\gamma}}\,H[\overline{\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}F}]\,H[\overline{\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}G}])}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,\rho\,d\,\overline{\eta})_{\overline{C}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\phi},\overline{\gamma}\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\mathit{id}_{\rho[\overline{\gamma:=C}]}[\overline{\phi:=\lambda\overline{B}.\eta_{\overline{B}\,\overline{C}}}]

is indeed a morphism of type

\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\gamma}\vdash H[\overline{\phi:=F}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\gamma:=C}]\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\gamma}\vdash H[\overline{\phi:=G}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\gamma:=C}]

This family of morphisms is natural in
\overline{C}
: if
\overline{f:C\to C^{\prime}}

then, writing
\xi
for

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{map}^{\overline{F},\overline{G}}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\;(\overline{\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta},\overline{\gamma}}\,F\,G})\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\gamma}}\,H[\overline{\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}F}]\,H[\overline{\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}G}])}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,\rho\,d\,\overline{\eta}

the naturality of
\eta
,
together with the fact that composition of environments is
computed componentwise, ensure that the following naturality diagram
for
\xi
commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\gamma}\vdash H[\overline{\phi:=F}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\gamma:=C}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\gamma}\vdash H[\overline{\phi:=G}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\gamma:=C}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\gamma}\vdash H[\overline{\phi:=F}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\gamma:=C^{\prime}}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\gamma}\vdash H[\overline{\phi:=G}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\gamma:=C^{\prime}}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{\overline{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\gamma}\vdash H[\overline{\phi:=F}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\mathit{id}_{\rho}[\overline{\gamma:=f}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\gamma}\vdash H[\overline{\phi:=G}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\mathit{id}_{\rho}[\overline{\gamma:=f}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{\overline{C^{\prime}}}}

That, for all
\rho:\mathsf{SetEnv}
and
d:\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\emptyset}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
,
\xi
satisfies the additional condition needed for
it to be in
\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\gamma}}\,H[\overline{\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}F}]\,H[\overline{\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}G}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
follows from the fact
that
\eta
satisfies the extra
condition needed for it to be in its corresponding

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta},\overline{\gamma}}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
.
Finally, since
\Phi=\emptyset
, the naturality of

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{map}^{\overline{F},\overline{G}}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\;(\overline{\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta},\overline{\gamma}}\,F\,G})\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\gamma}}\,H[\overline{\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}F}]\,H[\overline{\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}G}])}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho

in

is trivial.

•

\underline{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{in}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\;(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}}
To

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{in}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\;(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,\rho\,d

is in

, we first note that, for all
\overline{B}
,

\displaystyle(\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{in}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\;(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,\rho\,d)_{\overline{B}}\,

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle(\mathit{in}_{T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho}})_{\overline{B}}

maps

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash H[\phi:=(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\beta:=B}]

\displaystyle T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho}\,(\mu T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})\,\overline{B}

to
\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\beta:=B}]=(\mu T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})\,\overline{B}
.

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{in}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\;(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,\rho\,d

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\mathit{in}_{T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho}}

is natural in
\overline{B}
, since naturality of

\mathit{in}_{T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho}}

ensures that the following diagram commutes for all
\overline{f:B\to B^{\prime}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho}\,(\mu T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})\,\overline{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mu T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})\,\overline{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho}\,(\mu T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})\,\overline{B^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mu T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})\,\overline{B^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho}\,(\mu T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})\,\overline{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathit{in}_{T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})_{\overline{B}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu{T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho}}\,{\overline{f}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathit{in}_{T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho}})_{\overline{B^{\prime}}}}

That, for all
\rho:\mathsf{SetEnv}
and
d:\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\emptyset}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
,

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{in}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\;(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,\rho\,d

satisfies the additional property needed for
it to be in

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\;(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,\rho

follows from the fact that, for every
\overline{R:\mathsf{Rel}(B,B^{\prime})}
,

\hskip-14.45377pt\begin{array}[]{ll}&(\,(\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{in}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\;(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,\rho\,d)_{\overline{B}},\\
&\hskip 36.135pt(\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{in}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\;(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,\rho\,d)_{\overline{B^{\prime}}}\,)\\
&=(\,(\mathit{in}_{T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho}})_{\overline{B}},(\mathit{in}_{T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho}})_{\overline{B^{\prime}}}\,)\end{array}

has type

\begin{array}[]{ll}&(\,T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho}\,(\mu T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})\,\overline{B}\to(\mu T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})\,\overline{B},\,T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho}\,(\mu T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})\,\overline{B^{\prime}}\to(\mu T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})\,\overline{B^{\prime}}\,)\\[4.30554pt]
