=&\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash H[\phi:=(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\overline{\beta:=R}]\to\\
&\hskip 36.135pt\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\overline{\beta:=R}]\end{array}

Finally, since
\Phi=\emptyset
, naturality of

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{in}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\;(\mu\phi.\lambda{\overline{\alpha}}.H){\overline{\beta}}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

in
\rho
is trivial.

•

\underline{\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{fold}^{F}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}F][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\,F)\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{{\overline{\beta}}}\,(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}\;F)}
Since
\Phi
is empty, to see
that

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{fold}^{F}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}F][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\,F)\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{{\overline{\beta}}}\,(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}\;F)}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

is a
natural transformation from
\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\emptyset}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
to

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}F][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\,F)\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{{\overline{\beta}}}\,(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}\,F)}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

we need
only show that, for all
\rho:\mathsf{SetEnv}
, all
\eta:\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}F][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\,F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
, and the
unique
d:\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\emptyset}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
,

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{fold}^{F}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}F][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\,F)\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{{\overline{\beta}}}\,(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}\,F)}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,\rho\,d\,\eta

has type

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{{\overline{\beta}}}\,(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}\,F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,\rho
,
i.e., for any
\overline{B}
,

(\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{fold}^{F}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}F][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\,F)\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{{\overline{\beta}}}\,(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}\,F)}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,\rho\,d\,\eta)_{\overline{B}}

is a morphism from

\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\beta:=B}]\,=\,(\mu T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})_{\overline{B}}
to
\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\beta:=B}]
. To see this, note that
\eta
is a
natural transformation from

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\lambda\overline{B}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash H[\phi:=F][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\beta:=B}]\\
=&\lambda\overline{B}.\,T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho}\,(\lambda\overline{A}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\beta:=A}])\,\overline{B}\end{array}

to

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\lambda\overline{B}.\,(\lambda\overline{A}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\beta:=A}])\overline{B}\\
=&\lambda\overline{B}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\beta:=B}]\end{array}

and thus for each
\overline{B}
,

(\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{fold}^{F}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}F][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\,F)\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{{\overline{\beta}}}\,(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}\,F)}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,\rho\,d\,\eta)_{\overline{B}}

is a morphism from

\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\beta:=B}]\,=\,(\mu T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})_{\overline{B}}
to
\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\beta:=B}]
. To see that this family of morphisms is
natural in
\overline{B}
, we observe that the following diagram commutes
for all
\overline{f:B\to B^{\prime}}
’:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mu T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})\,\overline{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\beta:=B}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mu T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})\,\overline{B^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\beta:=B^{\prime}}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathit{fold}_{T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho}}\,\eta)_{\overline{B}}\,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mu T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho})\,\overline{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\mathit{id}_{\rho}[\overline{\beta:=f}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathit{fold}_{T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho}}\eta)_{\overline{B^{\prime}}}\,}

by naturality of
\mathit{fold}_{T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho}}\eta
. To see that,
for all
\rho:\mathsf{SetEnv}
,

\eta:\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}F][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\,F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho

and
d\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\emptyset}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho
,

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{fold}^{F}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}F][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\,F)\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{{\overline{\beta}}}\,(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}\,F)}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,\rho\,d\,\eta

satisfies the additional condition
needed to be in
\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{{\overline{\beta}}}\,(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}\;F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,\rho
, let
\overline{R:\mathsf{Rel}(B,B^{\prime})}
.
Since
\eta
satisfies the
additional condition needed to be in

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,(H[\phi:=F][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}])\,F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho

we know that

\begin{array}[]{ll}&(\,(\mathit{fold}_{T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho}}\eta)_{\overline{B}},\,(\mathit{fold}_{T^{\mathsf{Set}}_{H,\rho}}\eta)_{\overline{B^{\prime}}}\,)\end{array}

has type

\begin{array}[]{ll}&(\mu T_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}})\,\overline{R}\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\overline{\beta:=R}]\\
=&(\mu T_{H,\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}})\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash\beta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\overline{\beta:=R}]}\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\overline{\beta:=R}]\\
=&\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\overline{\beta:=R}]\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\beta}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\overline{\beta:=R}]\end{array}

Finally, since
\Phi=\emptyset
, naturality of

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{fold}^{F}_{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\beta}}\,H[\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}F][\overline{\alpha:=\beta}]\,F)\;(\mathsf{Nat}^{{\overline{\beta}}}\,(\mu\phi.\lambda\overline{\alpha}.H)\overline{\beta}\;F)}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

in
\rho
is trivial.∎

The following theorem is an immediate consequence of
Theorem
8
:

Theorem 9
.

If
\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:F
and
\rho\in\mathsf{RelEnv}
,
and if
(a,b)\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho
,
then

(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{1}\rho)\,a\,,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{2}\rho)\,b)\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho
.

Finally, the Abstraction Theorem is the instantiation of
Theorem
9
to closed terms of closed type:

Theorem 10

(Abstraction Theorem)
.

If
\vdash t:F
, then
(\llbracket{\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}},\llbracket{\vdash t:F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}})\in\llbracket{\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}
.

7.
Free Theorems for Nested Types

In this section we show how Theorem
8
and its
consequences can be used to prove free theorems. Those in
Sections
7.3
,
7.4
,
and
7.5
go beyond mere naturality. We also
show that we can extend short cut fusion for lists
[
GLP93
]
to
nested types, thereby formally proving correctness of the
categorically inspired theorem from
[
JG10
]
.

7.1.
Free Theorem for Type of Polymorphic
Bottom

Suppose
\vdash g:\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}\,\mathbb{1}\,\alpha
and
g^{\mathsf{Set}}=\llbracket{\vdash g:\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}\,\mathbb{1}\,\alpha}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
. Then
g^{\mathsf{Set}}
is a
natural transformation from the constantly
1
-valued functor to the
identity functor in
\mathsf{Set}
. In particular, for every
S:\mathsf{Set}
,

g^{\mathsf{Set}}_{S}:1\to S
. Note, however, that if
S=\emptyset
, then
there can be no such morphism, so no such natural transformation, and
thus no term
\vdash g:\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}\mathbb{1}\,\alpha
, can exist. That
is, our calculus admits no (non-terminating) terms with the closed
type
\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}\mathbb{1}\,\alpha
of the polymorphic bottom.

7.2.
Free Theorem for Type of Polymorphic
Identity

Suppose
\vdash g:\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}\,\alpha\,\alpha
and
g^{\mathsf{Set}}=\llbracket{\vdash g:\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}\,\alpha\,\alpha}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
. Then
g^{\mathsf{Set}}
is a
natural transformation from the identity functor on
\mathsf{Set}
to
itself. If
S
is any set, if
a
is any element of
S
, and if
K_{a}:S\to S
is the constantly
a
-valued morphism on
S
, then
naturality of
g^{\mathsf{Set}}
gives that
g^{\mathsf{Set}}_{S}\circ K_{a}=K_{a}\circ g^{\mathsf{Set}}_{S}
, i.e.,
g^{\mathsf{Set}}_{S}\,a=a
, i.e.,
g^{\mathsf{Set}}_{S}=\mathit{id}_{S}
. That
is,
g^{\mathsf{Set}}
is the identity natural transformation for the identity
functor on
\mathsf{Set}
. So every closed term
g
of closed type

\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}\alpha\,\alpha
always denotes the identity natural
transformation for the identity functor on
\mathsf{Set}
, i.e., every closed
term
g
of type
\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}\alpha\,\alpha
denotes the polymorphic
identity function.

7.3.
Standard Free Theorems for ADTs and Nested
Types

We can derive in our calculus even those free theorems for polymorphic
functions over ADTs that are not consequences of naturality. We can,
e.g., prove the free theorem for
\mathit{filter}
’s type as follows:

Theorem 11
.

If
g:A\to B
,
\rho\in\mathsf{RelEnv}
,
\rho\alpha=(A,B,\langle{g}\rangle)
,

(a,b)\in\llbracket{\alpha;\emptyset\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho
,
(s\circ g,s)\in\llbracket{\alpha;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\alpha\,\mathit{Bool}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho
, and

\mathit{filter}=\llbracket{\alpha;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}(\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\,\alpha\,\mathit{Bool})\,(\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}(List\,\alpha)\,(List\,\alpha))}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

for some
t
, then

\mathit{map}_{\mathit{List}}\,g\circ\mathit{filter}\,(\pi_{1}\rho)\,a\,(s\circ g)=\mathit{filter}\,(\pi_{2}\rho)\,b\,s\circ\mathit{map}_{\mathit{List}}\,g

Proof 7.1
.

By Theorem
10
,

(\mathit{filter}\,(\pi_{1}\rho)\,a,\mathit{filter}\,(\pi_{2}\rho)\,b)\in\llbracket{\alpha;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}(\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\,\alpha\,\mathit{Bool})\,(\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}(List\,\alpha)\,(List\,\alpha))}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho

so if
(s^{\prime},s)\in\llbracket{\alpha;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\alpha\,\mathit{Bool}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho=\rho\alpha\to\mathsf{Eq}_{\mathit{Bool}}
and
(xs^{\prime},xs)\in\llbracket{\alpha;\emptyset\vdash List\,\alpha}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho
then

(\mathit{filter}\,(\pi_{1}\rho)\,a\,s^{\prime}\,xs^{\prime},\mathit{filter}\,(\pi_{2}\rho)\,b\,s\,xs)\in\llbracket{\alpha;\emptyset\vdash\mathit{List}\,\alpha}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho

(16)

If
\rho\alpha=(A,B,\langle{g}\rangle)
, then
\llbracket{\alpha;\emptyset\vdash\mathit{List}\,\alpha}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho=\langle{\mathit{map}_{\mathit{List}}\,g}\rangle
by Lemma
6

and demotion. Moreover,
xs=\mathit{map}_{\mathit{List}}\,g\,xs^{\prime}
and
(s^{\prime},s)\in\langle{g}\rangle\to\mathsf{Eq}_{\mathit{Bool}}
, so
s^{\prime}=s\circ g
. The result
follows from
Equation
16
.∎

A similar proof establishes the analogous result for, say, generalized
rose trees.

Theorem 12
.

If
g:A\to B
,

F,G:\mathsf{Set}\to\mathsf{Set}
,

\eta:F\to G
in
\mathsf{Set}
,
\rho\in\mathsf{RelEnv}
,
\rho\alpha=(A,B,\langle{g}\rangle)
,
\rho\psi=(F,G,\langle{\eta}\rangle)
,
(a,b)\in\llbracket{\alpha,\psi;\emptyset\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho
,
(s\circ g,s)\in\llbracket{\alpha;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\alpha\,\mathit{Bool}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho
, and

\mathit{filter}=\llbracket{\alpha,\psi;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}(\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\,\alpha\,\mathit{Bool})\,(\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}(\mathit{GRose}\,\psi\,\alpha)\,(\mathit{GRose}\,\psi\,(\alpha+\mathbb{1})))}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

for some
t
, then

\mathit{map}_{\mathit{GRose}}\,\eta\,(g+1)\circ\mathit{filter}\,(\pi_{1}\rho)\,a\,(s\circ g)=\mathit{filter}\,(\pi_{2}\rho)\,b\,s\circ\mathit{map}_{\mathit{GRose}}\,\eta\,g

This is not surprising since rose trees are essentially
ADT-like. However, as noted in Section
2.2
, our calculus
cannot express the type of a polymorphic filter function for a proper
nested type.

7.4.
Short Cut Fusion for Lists

We can recover standard short cut fusion for lists
[
GLP93
]
in our
calculus:

Theorem 13
.

If
\vdash F
,
\vdash H
, and

\hat{g}\,=\,\llbracket{\beta;\emptyset\,|\,\emptyset\vdash g:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}(\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}(\mathbb{1}+F\times\beta)\,\beta)\,\beta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
for some
g
, and if
c\in\llbracket{\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,\times\,\llbracket{\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\to\llbracket{\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
and
n\in\llbracket{\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
, then

\mathit{fold}_{1+\llbracket{\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\times\_}\,n\,c\;(\hat{g}\;(\mathit{List}\,\llbracket{\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}})\,\mathit{nil}\,\mathit{cons})=\hat{g}\,\llbracket{\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,n\,c

Proof 7.2
.

Theorem
10
gives
that, for any
\rho\in\mathsf{RelEnv}
,

\begin{array}[]{lll}(\hat{g}\,(\pi_{1}\rho),\,\hat{g}\,(\pi_{2}\rho))&\in&\llbracket{\beta;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}(\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}(\mathbb{1}+F\times\beta)\,\beta)\,\beta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho\\
&\cong&(((\llbracket{\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho\times\rho\beta)\to\rho\beta)\times\rho\beta)\to\rho\beta\end{array}

so if
(c^{\prime},c)\in\llbracket{\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho\times\rho\beta\to\rho\beta
and
(n^{\prime},n)\in\rho\beta
then

(\hat{g}\,(\pi_{1}\rho)\,n^{\prime}\,c^{\prime},\hat{g}\,(\pi_{2}\rho)\,n\,c)\in\rho\beta
.
In addition,

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vdash\mathsf{fold}_{\mathbb{1}+F\times\beta}^{H}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}(\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}(\mathbb{1}+F\times H)\,H)\,(\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}(\mu\alpha.\mathbb{1}+F\times\alpha)\,H)}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\mathit{fold}_{1+\llbracket{\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\times\_}

so that if
c\in\llbracket{\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,\times\,\llbracket{\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\to\llbracket{\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
and
n\in\llbracket{\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
,
then

(n,c)\in\llbracket{\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}(\mathbb{1}+F\times H)\,H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

The instantiation

\begin{array}[]{lll}\pi_{1}\rho\beta&=&\llbracket{\vdash\mu\alpha.\mathbb{1}+F\times\alpha}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}=\mathit{List}\,\llbracket{\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\\
\pi_{2}\rho\beta&=&\llbracket{\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\\
\rho\beta&=&\langle{\mathit{fold}_{1+\llbracket{\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\times\_}\,n\,c}\rangle:\mathsf{Rel}(\pi_{1}\rho\beta,\pi_{2}\rho\beta)\\
c^{\prime}&=&\mathit{cons}\\
n^{\prime}&=&\mathit{nil}\end{array}

thus gives that

(\hat{g}\;(\mathit{List}\,\llbracket{\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}})\,\mathit{nil}\,\mathit{cons},\hat{g}\,\llbracket{\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,n\,c)\in\langle{\mathit{fold}_{1+\llbracket{\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\times\_}\,n\,c}\rangle

i.e.,

\mathit{fold}_{1+\llbracket{\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\times\_}\,n\,c\;(\hat{g}\;(\mathit{List}\,\llbracket{\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}})\,\mathit{nil}\,\mathit{cons})=\hat{g}\,\llbracket{\vdash H}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,n\,c

We can extend short cut fusion results to arbitrary ADTs, as
in
[
Joh02
,
Pit98
]
.

7.5.
Short Cut Fusion for Arbitrary Nested
Types

We can extend short cut fusion for lists
[
GLP93
]
to nested types,
thereby formally prove correctness of the categorically inspired
theorem from
[
JG10
]
. We have:

Theorem 14
.

If
\emptyset;\phi,\alpha\vdash F
,
\emptyset;\alpha\vdash K
,

H:[\mathsf{Set},\mathsf{Set}]\to[\mathsf{Set},\mathsf{Set}]
is defined by

\begin{array}[]{lll}H\,f\,x&=&\llbracket{\emptyset;\phi,\alpha\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}[\phi:=f][\alpha:=x]\\
\end{array}

and

\hat{g}=\llbracket{\phi;\emptyset\,|\,\emptyset\vdash g:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\,(\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}\,F\,(\phi\alpha))\,(\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}\,\mathbb{1}\,(\phi\alpha))}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}

for some
g
, then, for every
B\in H\llbracket{\emptyset;\alpha\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rightarrow\llbracket{\emptyset;\alpha\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
,

\mathit{fold}_{H}\,B\,(\hat{g}\;\mu H\;\mathit{in}_{H})=\hat{g}\,\llbracket{\emptyset;\alpha\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,B

Proof 7.3
.

Theorem
10
gives that, for any

\rho\in\mathsf{RelEnv}
,

\begin{array}[]{lll}(\hat{g}\,(\pi_{1}\rho),\hat{g}\,(\pi_{2}\rho))&\in&\llbracket{\phi;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}(\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}F\,(\phi\alpha))\,(\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}\,\mathbb{1}\,(\phi\alpha))}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho\\
&=&\llbracket{\phi;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}F\,(\phi\alpha)}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho\to\llbracket{\phi;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}\,\mathbb{1}\,(\phi\alpha)}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho\\
&=&\llbracket{\phi;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}F\,(\phi\alpha)}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho\to\rho\phi\end{array}

so if
(A,B)\in\llbracket{\phi;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}F\,(\phi\alpha)}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho
then
(\hat{g}\,(\pi_{1}\rho)\,A,\hat{g}\,(\pi_{2}\rho)\,B)\in\rho\phi
.
Also,

\llbracket{\vdash\mathsf{fold}_{F}^{K}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\emptyset}\,(\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}F[\phi:=K]\,K)\,(\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}((\mu\phi.\lambda\alpha.F)\alpha)\,K)}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}=\mathit{fold}_{H}

Now let
A=\mathit{in}_{H}:H(\mu H)\Rightarrow\mu H
,
B:H\llbracket{\emptyset;\alpha\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\Rightarrow\llbracket{\emptyset;\alpha\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
,
\rho\phi=\langle{\mathit{fold}_{H}\,B}\rangle
,
\pi_{1}\rho\phi=\mu H
,
\pi_{2}\rho\phi=\llbracket{\emptyset;\alpha\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
,
\rho\phi:\mathsf{Rel}(\pi_{1}\rho\phi,\pi_{2}\rho\phi)
,
A:\llbracket{\phi;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}F\,(\phi\alpha)}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{1}\rho)
,
and
B:\llbracket{\phi;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}F\,(\phi\alpha)}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{2}\rho)
.
Demotion ensures that

A=\mathit{in}_{H}:H(\mu H)\Rightarrow\mu H=\llbracket{\phi;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}F\,(\phi\alpha)}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{1}\rho)

and demotion and Lemma
6
together give that

\begin{array}[]{lll}(A,B)\,=\,(\mathit{in}_{H},B)&\in&\llbracket{\phi;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\alpha}F\,(\phi\alpha)}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho\\
&=&\lambda A.\llbracket{\phi;\alpha\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}[\phi:=\langle{\mathit{fold}_{H}\,B}\rangle][\alpha:=A]\Rightarrow\langle{\mathit{fold}_{H}\,B}\rangle\\
&=&\llbracket{\emptyset;\phi,\alpha\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\langle{\mathit{fold}_{H}\,B}\rangle\Rightarrow\langle{\mathit{fold}_{H}\,B}\rangle\\
&=&\langle{\llbracket{\emptyset;\phi,\alpha\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,(\mathit{fold}_{H}\,B)}\rangle\Rightarrow\langle{\mathit{fold}_{H}\,B}\rangle\\
&=&\langle{\mathit{map}_{H}\,(\mathit{fold}_{H}\,B)}\rangle\Rightarrow\langle{\mathit{fold}_{H}\,B}\rangle\\
\end{array}

since if
(x,y)\in\langle{\mathit{map}_{H}\,(\mathit{fold}_{H}\,B)}\rangle
,
then

\mathit{fold}_{H}\,B\,(\mathit{in}_{H}\,x)=B\,y=B\,(\mathit{map}_{H}\,(\mathit{fold}_{H}\,B)\,x)

by the definition of

\mathit{fold}_{H}
as a (indeed, the unique) morphism from

\mathit{in}_{H}
to
B
. Thus,

(\hat{g}\,(\pi_{1}\rho)\,A,\hat{g}\,(\pi_{2}\rho)\,B)\in\langle{\mathit{fold}_{H}\,B}\rangle,

i.e.,
\mathit{fold}_{H}\,B\,(\hat{g}\,(\pi_{1}\rho)\,\mathit{in}_{H})=\hat{g}\,(\pi_{2}\rho)\,B
. But
since
\phi
is the only free variable in
\hat{g}
, this simplifies to

\mathit{fold}_{H}\,B\,(\hat{g}\,\mu H\,\mathit{in}_{H})=\hat{g}\,\llbracket{\emptyset;\alpha\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,B
. ∎

As in
[
JG10
]
, replacing
\mathbb{1}
with any type
\emptyset;\alpha\vdash C
generalizes Theorem
14
to deliver a
more general free theorem whose conclusion is
\mathit{fold}_{H}\,B\;\circ\;\hat{g}\;\mu H\;\mathit{in}_{H}=\hat{g}\,\llbracket{\emptyset;\alpha\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\,B
.

Although it is standard to prove that the parametric model constructed
verifies the existence of initial algebras, this is unnecessary here
since initial algebras are built directly into our model.

8.
Parametricity for GADTs

As discussed in Section
1
, type indices for nested types
can be any types, including, in the case of truly nested types like
that of bushes, types involving the very same nested type that is
being defined. But every data constructor for a nested type must still
have as its return type exactly the instance being defined. For
example, the data constructors for the instance
\mathtt{PTree\;A}
of the
nested type
\mathtt{PTree}
are
\mathtt{pleaf::PTree\;A}
and

\mathtt{pnode::PTree\;(A\times A)\to PTree\;A}
, and
the data constructors for the instance
\mathtt{Bush\;A}
of the truly
nested type
\mathtt{Bush}
are
\mathtt{bnil::Bush\;A}
and

\mathtt{bcons::A\to Bush\;(Bush\;A)\to Bush\;A}
.

Generalized algebraic data types (GADTs) — also known as guarded
recursive data types
[
XCC03
]
or first-class phantom
types
[
CH03
]
— generalize nested types by relaxing the above
restriction to allow the return types of data constructors to be
different instances of the data type than the one being defined. For
example, the GADT

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{data\;Seq\;(A:Set)\;:\;Set\;where}\\
\hskip 28.90755pt\mathtt{sconst\;:\;A\rightarrow Seq\;A}\\
\hskip 28.90755pt\mathtt{spair\;:\;Seq\;A\rightarrow Seq\;B\rightarrow Seq\;(A\times B)}\\
\hskip 28.90755pt\mathtt{sseq\;:\;(Nat\rightarrow Seq\;A)\rightarrow Seq\;(Nat\to A)}\end{array}

has data constructors
\mathtt{spair}
and
\mathtt{sseq}
with
return types
\mathtt{Seq\;(A\times B)}
and
\mathtt{Seq\;(Nat\to A)}
. These types are not only at different instances of
Seq

from the instance
\mathtt{Seq\;A}
being defined, but also at
different instances from one another. The resulting interdependence of
different instances of GADTs means that they can express more
constraints than ADTs and nested types. For example, the ADT

\mathtt{List}
expresses the invariant that all of the data in the
lists it defines is of the same type, while the nested type

\mathtt{PTree}
expresses this invariant
as well as
the
invariant that all of the lists it defines have lengths that are
powers of 2. The GADT
\mathtt{Seq}
enforces
even more general

well-formedness conditions for sequences of values that simply cannot
be expressed with ADTs and nested types alone.

GADTs are widely used in modern functional languages, such as Haskell,
as well as in proof assistants, such as Agda, that are based on
dependent type theories. A natural next step in the line of work
reported in this paper is therefore to extend our parametricity
results to GADTs. A promising starting point for this endeavor is the
observation from
[
JG08
]
that the data objects of GADTs can be
represented using object-level left Kan extensions over discrete
categories. The more recent results of
[
JP19
]
further show that
adding a carefully designed object-level left Kan extension construct
to a calculus supporting primitive nested types preserves the
cocontinuity needed for primitive GADTs to have well-defined, properly
functorial interpretations. Together this suggests extending the type
system in Definition
2.1
with such a left Kan extension
construct, and extending the calculus in Figure
1
with
corresponding categorically inspired constructs to introduce and
eliminate terms of these richer types. This approach exactly mirrors
the (entirely standard) approach taken above for product, coproduct,
and fixpoint types. In this section we outline the obvious approach to
extending our model from Section
5
to a parametric
model when some classes of primitive GADTs are incorporated in this
manner. However, as we argue at the end of this section, this naive
approach fails because the IEL does not hold. Unfortunately, the IEL
not holding derails more than just parametricity: it also affects the
well-definedness of the term semantics (in particular, the semantics
of
L
-terms).

8.1.
Left Kan Extensions

We begin by recalling the definition of a left Kan extension and
establishing some useful notation and results for them.

{defi}

If
F:\mathsf{Set}^{k}\to\mathsf{Set}
and
\overline{K}:\mathsf{Set}^{k}\to\mathsf{Set}^{h}
are functors
over
\mathsf{Set}
, then the
left Kan extension of
F
along
\overline{K}
is
a functor
\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}\,F:\mathsf{Set}^{h}\to\mathsf{Set}
together with a
natural transformation
\eta:F\to(\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}\,F)\circ\overline{K}

such that, for every functor
G:\mathsf{Set}^{h}\to\mathsf{Set}
and natural
transformation
\gamma:F\to G\circ\overline{K}
, there exists a unique
natural transformation
\mu:\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}\,F\to G
such that

(\mu{\overline{K}})\circ\eta=\gamma
. This is depicted in the
following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Set}^{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Set}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Set}^{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}\,F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}

Replacing
\mathsf{Set}
by
\mathsf{Rel}
everywhere in Definition
8.1
we
can similarly define the left Kan extension of
F
along
\overline{K}
for
functors
F
and
\overline{K}
over
\mathsf{Rel}
.

An alternative presentation characterizes the left Kan extension

(\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F,\eta)
in terms of the bijection between
natural transformations from
F
to
G\circ\overline{K}
and natural
transformations from
\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F
to
G
, for which

\eta
is the unit. If Agda were to support a primitive

\mathsf{Lan}
for left Kan extensions, we could use this bijection
to rewrite the type of each data constructor for the GADT

\mathtt{Seq}
to arrive at the following equivalent representation:

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{data\;Seq\;(A:Set)\;:\;Set\;where}\\
\hskip 28.90755pt\mathtt{sconst\;:\;A\rightarrow Seq\;A}\\
\hskip 28.90755pt\mathtt{spair\;:\;(\mathsf{Lan}_{\lambda C\,D.\,C\times D}(\lambda C\,D.\,Seq\;C\times Seq\;D))\;A\rightarrow Seq\;A}\\
\hskip 28.90755pt\mathtt{sseq\;:\;(\mathsf{Lan}_{\lambda C.\,Nat\to C}(\lambda C.\,Nat\rightarrow Seq\;C))\;A\rightarrow Seq\;A}\end{array}

​​Our calculus will represent
\mathtt{Seq}
and other GADTs in
precisely this way.

A third representation of left Kan extensions in locally presentable
categories is given in terms of colimits. Writing
{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
for
the full subcategory of finitely presentable objects in the locally
presentable category
\mathcal{C}
, the left Kan extension can be
expressed as

(\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}\;=\;{{\underrightarrow{\lim}}_{\overline{X:\,{\mathcal{C}}_{0}},\,\overline{f:K{\overline{X}}\to A}}{F\overline{X}}}

Thus, if
F:\mathsf{Set}^{k}\to\mathsf{Set}
,
\overline{K}:\mathsf{Set}^{k}\to\mathsf{Set}^{h}
,
\overline{A}:\mathsf{Set}^{h}
, and
\mathsf{Set}_{0}
is the full
subcategory of finitely presentable objects in
\mathsf{Set}
, — i.e., is
the category of finite sets — we have that

(\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}={{\underrightarrow{\lim}}_{\overline{S}:\mathsf{Set}_{0}^{k},\,\overline{f:K\overline{S}\to A}}{F\overline{S}}}

(17)

If
\overline{S}:\mathsf{Set}_{0}^{k}
and
\overline{f:K\overline{S}\to A}
, let
j_{\overline{S},\overline{f}}:F\overline{S}\to(\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}
be the morphism
indexed by
\overline{S}
and
\overline{f}
mapping the cocone into the colimit
in Equation
17
.
For any
h
-tuple of functions

\overline{g:A\to B}
, the functorial action
(\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{g}

is the unique function from
(\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}
to

(\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{B}
such that, for all
\overline{S}:\mathsf{Set}^{k}_{0}
and

\overline{f:K\overline{S}\to A}
,

(\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{g}\circ j_{\overline{S},\,\overline{f}}=j^{\prime}_{\overline{S},\,\overline{g\,\circ f}}:F\overline{S}\to(\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{B}

(18)

holds, where
j
is the morphism mapping the cocone into

(\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}={{\underrightarrow{\lim}}_{\overline{S}:\mathsf{Set}_{0}^{k},\,\overline{f:K\overline{S}\to A}}{F\overline{S}}}
and
j^{\prime}
is the morphism mapping the
cocone into
(\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{B}={{\underrightarrow{\lim}}_{\overline{S}:\mathsf{Set}_{0}^{k},\,\overline{f:K\overline{S}\to B}}{F\overline{S}}}
. Moreover, if
\alpha:F\to F^{\prime}
is a natural transformation, then
\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}\alpha:(\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F)\to(\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F^{\prime})
is defined to be the
induced natural transformation whose component
(\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}\alpha)\overline{A}:(\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}\to(\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F^{\prime})\overline{A}
is the unique function such that
(\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}\alpha)\overline{A}\,\circ\,j_{\overline{S},\overline{f}}=j^{\prime}_{\overline{S},\overline{f}}\,\circ\,\alpha_{\overline{S}}
, where
j
is the cocone into
(\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}
and
j^{\prime}
is the cocone into
(\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F^{\prime})\overline{A}
. We
can similarly represent left Kan extensions of functors over
\mathsf{Rel}
in
terms of colimits. In that setting we will denote the morphism mapping
the cocone into the colimit
\iota
. We will make good use of both of
these representations in Section
8.4
below.

More about each of the above three representations of left Kan
extensions and the connections between them can be found in,
e.g.,
[
Rie16
]
.

8.2.
Extending the Calculus

We incorporate GADTs into our calculus by first adding to
Definition
2.1
the following type formation rule for

\mathsf{Lan}
-types:

\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}^{0}\vdash F

\overline{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}^{0}\vdash K}

\overline{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}

\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha^{0}}}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}

Here, the type constructor
\mathsf{Lan}
binds the variables in
\overline{\alpha}
,
and these variables must always have arity
0
. In addition, the
vectors
\overline{K}
and
\overline{A}
must have the same length.

Intuitively,
\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha^{0}}}_{\overline{K}}F
is a syntactic representation
of the left Kan extension of the functor in the variables in

\overline{\alpha}
denoted by
F
along the functor in the variables in

\overline{\alpha}
denoted by
\overline{K}
. Using
\mathsf{Lan}
-types, we can therefore
represent the GADT
\mathtt{Seq}
in our calculus as

\displaystyle\mathtt{Seq}\,\alpha

\displaystyle\;:=\;\left(\mu\phi.\lambda\beta.\beta+\left(\mathsf{Lan}^{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}_{\gamma_{1}\times\gamma_{2}}(\phi\gamma_{1}\times\phi\gamma_{2})\right)\beta\right.\left.+\left(\mathsf{Lan}^{\gamma}_{\mathtt{Nat}\to\gamma}(\mathtt{Nat}\to\phi\gamma)\right)\beta\right)\alpha

where
\mathtt{Nat}=\mu\alpha.1+\alpha
and
\mathtt{Nat}\to C

abbreviates
(\mathsf{Lan}^{\emptyset}_{\mathtt{Nat}}1)\,C
, which equals

{{\underrightarrow{\lim}}_{f:\mathtt{Nat}\to C}{1}}
by Equation
17

(here,
k=0
). This representation of
\mathit{Seq}
corresponds
exactly to the rewriting in Section
8.1
. As explained in
Section IV. D of
[
JP19
]
, the more general construct

\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha^{0}}}_{\overline{K}}F
allowing extensions along vectors of
functors as depicted above makes it possible to represent GADTs with
two or more type arguments that depend on one another. Such GADTs
cannot be represented using just unary
\mathsf{Lan}
-types, i.e,
\mathsf{Lan}
-types
of the form
(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha^{0}}}_{K}F)A
.

As we will see below, in order to define the term-formation rules for
terms of
\mathsf{Lan}
-types we also need to generalize
\mathsf{Nat}
-types to bind
type constructor variables not just of arity
0
, but of arbitrary
arity. Accordingly, we replace the rule for
\mathsf{Nat}
-types in
Definition
2.1
with

\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F

\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G

\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\Phi}F\,G

Of course, we could have performed this replacement from the outset of
the present paper. But since using the above rule instead of the one
currently in Definition
2.1
only makes the notation of
our calculus heavier without increasing its expressivity, we have
chosen not to.

Finally, adding
\mathsf{Lan}
-types to the types of our calculus also
requires the extension of Definition
2.1
with
the following new clause:

((\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}\,F)\overline{A})[\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}G]=(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}\,F[\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}G])\overline{A[\phi:=_{\overline{\beta}}G]}

We must also extend our term calculus to accommodate GADTs. To this
end, we first give introduction and elimination rules appropriate to
our generalized
\mathsf{Nat}
-types, replacing the tenth and eleventh rules
in Figure
1
with

\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F

\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G

\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta,x:F\vdash t:G

\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash L_{\Phi}x.t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\Phi}\,F\,G

and

\overline{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\beta}\vdash K}

\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\overline{\psi}}\,F\,G

\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash s:F[\overline{\psi:=_{\overline{\beta}}K}]

\Gamma;\Phi\,|\,\Delta\vdash t_{\overline{K}}s:G[\overline{\psi:=_{\overline{\beta}}K}]

respectively. Next, we add to the term calculus in
Figure
1
introduction and elimination rules for terms of

\mathsf{Lan}
-types according to Definition
8.1
. This gives the
rules

\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F

\overline{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}

\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\emptyset\vdash\int_{\overline{K},F}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\Phi,\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}\,F)\overline{K}

and

\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\Delta\vdash t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\Phi,\overline{\alpha}}\,F\;G[\overline{\beta:=K}]

\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\Delta\vdash\partial^{G,\overline{K}}_{F}t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\Phi,\overline{\beta}}\,(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}\,F)\overline{\beta}\,G

respectively. Note that both of these rules make essential use of our
generalized
\mathsf{Nat}
-types. If
\Phi
were required to be empty then we
would not be able to express, e.g., the types

\mathsf{Nat}^{\phi,\gamma,\delta}(\phi\gamma\times\phi\delta)\;(\phi(\gamma\times\phi))

and

\mathsf{Nat}^{\phi,\beta}\,(\mathsf{Lan}^{\gamma,\delta}_{\gamma\times\delta}\,(\phi\gamma\times\phi\delta))\beta\;(\phi\beta)

associated with
\mathtt{Seq}
’s data constructor
\mathtt{spair}
.

8.3.
Extending the Type Semantics

To construct a parametric model of the kind we seek, we must give
suitable interpretations of our generalized
\mathsf{Nat}
-types and

\mathsf{Lan}
-types, as well as of terms of both such types, in both
\mathsf{Set}

and
\mathsf{Rel}
. (Note that it is having both
\mathsf{Set}
and

\mathsf{Rel}
interpretations, and having these be interconnected in
the specific way parametricity demands, that is at issue here; if we
are only interested in having
some
model of the type calculus,
then the one in
[
JP19
]
will do.) Recalling that the type
constructor variables bound by
\mathsf{Nat}
in the formation rule for
generalized
\mathsf{Nat}
-types are interpreted in
\mathsf{Set}
as functors and in

\mathsf{Rel}
as relation transformers, we let
k_{i}
be the arity of the

i^{th}
element of the sequence
\Phi
and extend the set and
relational interpretations for
\mathsf{Nat}
types from
Section
3
as follows:

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\Phi}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho

\displaystyle=\{\eta:\lambda\overline{K}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\Phi:=\overline{K}]\Rightarrow\lambda\overline{K}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\Phi:=\overline{K}]~|~

\displaystyle\hskip 21.68121pt\forall\overline{K=(K^{1},K^{2},K^{*}):RT_{k}}.

\displaystyle\hskip 28.90755pt(\eta_{\overline{K^{1}}},\eta_{\overline{K^{2}}}):\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\Phi:=\overline{K}]\rightarrow\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathsf{Eq}_{\rho}[\Phi:=\overline{K}]\}

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\Phi}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho

\displaystyle=\{\eta:\lambda\overline{K}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\Phi:=\overline{K}]\Rightarrow\lambda\overline{K}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\Phi:=\overline{K}]\}

\displaystyle=\{(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})\in\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\Phi}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{1}\rho)\times\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\mathsf{Nat}^{\Phi}\,F\,G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{2}\rho)~|~

\displaystyle\hskip 21.68121pt\forall\overline{K=(K^{1},K^{2},K^{*}):RT_{k}}.

\displaystyle\hskip 28.90755pt((\eta_{1})_{\overline{K^{1}}},(\eta_{2})_{\overline{K^{2}}})\in(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\Phi:=\overline{K}])^{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\Phi:=\overline{K}]}\}

Of course, we intend to interpret
\mathsf{Lan}
-types as actual left Kan
extensions. To get started, we define their set and relational
interpretations to be

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}\,F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho=(\textit{Lan}_{\lambda\overline{S}.\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=S}]}}\,\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=S}])\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}

and

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}\,F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho=(\textit{Lan}_{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}])\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho}

respectively. But in order to guarantee that the IEL, and thus
parametricity, continues to hold for the model we are constructing for
our extended calculus, we might think we have to cut down the set
interpretations of
\mathsf{Lan}
-types by restricting to the subcollection of
those interpretations that are well-behaved with respect to the
IEL. This would mirror the cutting down we have already seen in the
set interpretations of both our original
\mathsf{Nat}
-types and our of
generalized
\mathsf{Nat}
-types. However, as we will see in
Proposition
15
below, this will not be possible if we
want the set and relational interpretations of our
\mathsf{Lan}
-types to
include the set and relational interpretations of the terms given by
the
\mathsf{Lan}
-introduction rule. Since we do indeed want this, the upshot
is that the above interpretations of
\mathsf{Lan}
-types are, in fact, the
only possibility.

We must also define the functorial actions of the above set and
relational interpretations of
\mathsf{Lan}
-types on morphisms. If
f:\rho\to\rho^{\prime}
is a morphism of set environments, then

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}\,F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f:\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}\,F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}\,F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho^{\prime}

is defined to be

\big(\textit{Lan}_{\lambda\overline{S}.\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=S}]}}\,\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{S}}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho^{\prime}}\\
\circ\;\big(\textit{Lan}_{\lambda\overline{S}.\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=S}]}}\,\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=S}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f}

or, equivalently by naturality,

\big(\textit{Lan}_{\lambda\overline{S}.\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=S}]}}\,\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho^{\prime}[\overline{\alpha:=S}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f}\\
\circ\;\big(\textit{Lan}_{\lambda\overline{S}.\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=S}]}}\,\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{S}}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho}

The functorial action for the relational interpretations of

\mathsf{Lan}
-types is defined analogously.

8.4.
Extending the Term Semantics

Recalling the notation for the colimit representation of left Kan
extensions from Section
8.1
, we can define the set and
relational interpretations of the terms from the introduction and
elimination rules for
\mathsf{Lan}
types from Section
8.2
by

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\emptyset\vdash\int_{\overline{K},F}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\Phi,\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}\,F)\overline{K}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\,d=\eta

and

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\Delta\vdash\partial^{G,\overline{K}}_{F}t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\Phi,\overline{\beta}}\,(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}\,F)\overline{\beta}\;G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho=\mu

respectively, where, for each
\overline{N:RT_{k}}
,
\eta_{\overline{N}}

is the natural transformation associated with the left Kan extension

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Set}^{|\overline{\alpha}|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Set}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Set}^{|\overline{K}|}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda\overline{A}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho{[\Phi,\overline{\alpha}:=\overline{N},\overline{A}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\lambda\overline{A}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho{[\overline{\alpha:=A}]}}\;\;\;\;}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\;\;\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{A}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho{[\overline{\alpha:=A}]}}}\,\lambda\overline{A}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho{[\Phi,\overline{\alpha}:=\overline{N},\overline{A}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\overline{N}}}

and, for all
\overline{N:RT_{k}}
and
d:\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\vdash\Delta}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
,

\begin{array}[]{l}\mu_{\overline{N}\,d}:\mathit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{A}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho{[\overline{\alpha:=A}]}}}\lambda\overline{A}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho{[\Phi,\overline{\alpha}:=\overline{N},\overline{A}]}\\[8.61108pt]
\hskip 57.81621pt\to\lambda\overline{B}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\beta}\vdash G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\Phi,\overline{\beta}:=\overline{N},\overline{B}]\end{array}

is the unique natural transformation such that

(\mu_{\overline{N}\,d}\,\overline{\lambda\overline{A}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=A}]})\circ\eta_{\overline{N}}=(\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset\,|\,\Delta\vdash t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\Phi,\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,\,G[\overline{\beta:=K}]}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\,d)_{\overline{N}}

(19)

as given by the universal property of the left Kan extension. The
relational interpretations

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\emptyset\vdash\int_{\overline{K},F}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\Phi,\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}\,F)\overline{K}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}
and

\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\Delta\vdash\partial^{G,\overline{K}}_{F}t:\mathsf{Nat}^{\Phi,\overline{\beta}}\,(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}\,F)\overline{\beta}\;G}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}
can then be defined entirely analogously.

We conclude this subsection by showing, as promised above, that no
cutting down of set and relational interpretations of
\mathsf{Lan}
-types by
taking subsets is possible. We have:

Proposition 15
.

Let
F:\mathsf{Set}^{k}\to\mathsf{Set}
and
\overline{K}:\mathsf{Set}^{k}\to\mathsf{Set}^{h}
be functors.
If
L:\mathsf{Set}^{h}\to\mathsf{Set}
is a functor such that

•

L\overline{A}\subseteq(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}
for all
\overline{A:\mathsf{Set}}
,

•

(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{f}\,|_{L\overline{A}}\;x\in L\overline{B}
for all
\overline{f:A\to B}
and
x\in L\overline{A}
, and

•

\eta_{\overline{A}}\,y\in L(\overline{K\overline{A}})
for all
\overline{A:\mathsf{Set}}
and
y\in F\overline{A}
,

then
L=\textit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F
.

Proof 8.1
.

For all
\overline{A:\mathsf{Set}}
and
z\in(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}

there exist
\overline{S:\mathsf{Set}_{0}}
,
\overline{f:K\overline{S}\to A}
, and
w\in F\overline{S}
such that
z=\iota_{\overline{S},\overline{f}}\,w
. Thus,
z=\iota_{\overline{S},\overline{f}}\,w=(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{f}(\eta_{\overline{S}}\,w)
by Equation
18
. Then by the
third assumption above we have that
\eta_{\overline{S}}\,w\in L(\overline{K\overline{S}})
, and by the second assumption above we have that
z\in L\overline{A}
. This gives
(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}\subseteq L\overline{A}
. Finally, by the first assumption above, we therefore have that
L\overline{A}=(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}
.

Thus, if
L
were a restriction of
\lambda\overline{B}.\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\beta}\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F){\overline{\beta}}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\Phi:=\overline{N}][\overline{\beta:=B}]
,
if the functorial
action of
L
were a restriction of that of
\lambda\overline{B}.\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\beta}\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F){\overline{\beta}}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\Phi:=\overline{N}][\overline{\beta:=B}]
, and if

L(\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=A}]})

contained
\llbracket{\Gamma;\emptyset~|~\emptyset\vdash\!\int_{\overline{K},F}:\mathsf{Nat}^{\Phi,\overline{\alpha}}\,F\,(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}\,F)\overline{K}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho\,d\,\overline{N}\,\overline{A}\,y
for all
\overline{N}
,
\overline{A}
, and

y
, then
L
would have to be the entirety of

\lambda\overline{B}.\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\beta}\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F){\overline{\beta}}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\Phi:=\overline{N}][\overline{\beta:=B}]
.
An analogous result holds for relational interpretations of

\mathsf{Lan}
-types. This shows that the machinery of this section does not
give a well-defined relational semantics and, given the expected
properties of the term semantics, this cannot be
fixed by restricting the relational semantics of
\mathsf{Lan}
-types
from Section
8.3
.

8.5.
Parametricity and GADTs

Having extended our calculus with both
\mathsf{Lan}
-types and terms of such
types, and having given sensible set and relational interpretations
for these types and terms, we now need to verify that these
interpretations give rise to a parametric model. The first step in
this process is to extend Lemma
2
to

\mathsf{Lan}
-types by adding a clause for
\mathsf{Lan}
-types to the proof.
Unfortunately, however, Lemma
2
does not
extend to arbitrary
\mathsf{Lan}
-types, as the following example shows.

{exa}

Consider the type
\emptyset;\alpha\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\emptyset}_{\mathbb{1}}\,\mathbb{1})\alpha
. The analogue for
\mathsf{Rel}
of
Equation
17
gives

\begin{array}[]{rl}\llbracket{\emptyset;\alpha\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\emptyset}_{\mathbb{1}}\mathbb{1})\alpha}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\alpha:=(1,0,0)]&=(\textit{Lan}_{\mathsf{Eq}_{1}}\mathsf{Eq}_{1})(1,0,0)\\
&={{\underrightarrow{\lim}}_{f:\mathsf{Eq}_{1}\to(1,0,0)}{\mathsf{Eq}_{1}}}\\
&=(0,0,0)\end{array}

Here, the last equality holds because there are no morphisms in
\mathsf{Rel}

from
\mathsf{Eq}_{1}
to
(1,0,0)
, and because
(0,0,0)
is the initial
object in that category. On the other hand, for the set interpretation
with respect to the first projection of the relation environment

\rho[\alpha:=(1,0,0)]
, Equation
17
gives

\begin{array}[]{rl}\llbracket{\emptyset;\alpha\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\emptyset}_{\mathbb{1}}\mathbb{1})\alpha}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{1}(\rho[\alpha:=(1,0,0)]))&=\llbracket{\emptyset;\alpha\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\emptyset}_{\mathbb{1}}\mathbb{1})\alpha}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{1}\rho)[\alpha:=1]\\
&=(\textit{Lan}_{1}1)1\\
&={{\underrightarrow{\lim}}_{1\to 1}{1}}\\
&=1\end{array}

because
\mathit{id}_{1}
is the unique arrow from
1
to
1
in
\mathsf{Set}
.
Lemma
2
therefore cannot hold.

The problem in Example
8.5
lies in the fact that
the type along whose interpretation we extend contains constants, i.e.,
subtypes constructed from
\mathbb{1}
. Indeed, if
\overline{\emptyset;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}
consists only of polynomial (i.e.,
sum-of-products) types not containing constants (i.e., formed only
from
+
,
\times
, and the variables in
\alpha
), then
Lemma
2
actually does holds for
\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\alpha}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}
. This is proved in the
following proposition, which covers the case of
Lemma
2
for such
\mathsf{Lan}
-types.

Proposition 16
.

If
\overline{\emptyset;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}
consists only of
polynomial types not containing constants, then

(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}},\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}},\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}})

is an
\omega
-cocontinuous environment transformer.

Proof 8.2
.

We need only show that

\pi_{i}\,(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho)=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)

(20)

and

\pi_{i}\,(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f)=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}f)

(21)

for all
\rho
,
\rho^{\prime}
,
f:\rho\to\rho^{\prime}
, and
i\in\{1,2\}
.

To prove Equation
20
we first observe that, by (the
analogue for
\mathsf{Rel}
of) Equation
17
, we have

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho={{\underrightarrow{\lim}}_{\overline{R:\mathsf{Rel}_{0}},\,\overline{m:\llbracket{\emptyset;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho}}{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}

with
\iota
mapping the cocone into this colimit. Since each
projection
\pi_{i}
is cocontinuous, we therefore have that

\displaystyle\pi_{i}\,(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho)

\displaystyle\quad{}={{\underrightarrow{\lim}}_{\overline{R:\mathsf{Rel}_{0}},\overline{m:\llbracket{\emptyset;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho}}{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=\pi_{i}R}]}}

with
\pi_{i}\iota
mapping the cocone into this colimit. On the
other hand, Equation
17
also gives

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)

\displaystyle\quad{}={{\underrightarrow{\lim}}_{\overline{S:\mathsf{Set}_{0}},\overline{f:\llbracket{\emptyset;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=S}]\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)}}{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=S}]}}

with
j
mapping the cocone into this colimit.

When
i=1
we prove that

\pi_{1}\,(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho)=\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{1}\rho)

by
providing a pair of inverse functions; the proof when
i=2
is
entirely analogous. To this end, we define

h:\pi_{1}\,(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho)\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{1}\rho)

to be the unique function given by the universal property of the
colimit representation of
h
’s domain. That is, we take
h
to be the
unique morphism such that, for any
\overline{R:\mathsf{Rel}_{0}}
and

\overline{m:\llbracket{\emptyset;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho}

we have

h\circ(\pi_{1}\iota_{\overline{R},\,\overline{m}})=j_{\overline{\pi_{1}R},\,\overline{\pi_{1}m}}

(22)

Now, since
K
does not contain constants,
\llbracket{\emptyset;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{2}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=0}]=0
. Thus, for
any
\overline{S:\mathsf{Set}_{0}}
and
\overline{f:\llbracket{\emptyset;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{1}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=S}]\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{1}\rho)}
we have relations
\overline{(S,0,0)}

and morphisms
\overline{(f,!):\llbracket{\emptyset;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=(S,0,0)}]\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho}
, where, for any set
X
, we suppress the sub- and
superscripts and write
!
for the unique morphism
!^{0}_{X}
from
0
to

X
. We then define

k:\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{1}\rho)\to\pi_{1}\,(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho)

to be the unique function given by the universal property of the
colimit representation of
k
’s domain. That is, we take
k
to be the
unique morphism such that, for any
\overline{S:\mathsf{Set}_{0}}
and

\overline{f:\llbracket{\emptyset;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{1}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=S}]\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{1}\rho)}

we have

k\circ j_{\overline{S},\,\overline{f}}=\pi_{1}\iota_{\overline{(S,0,0)},\,\overline{(f,!)}}
.

To see that
h
and
k
are mutually inverse we first observe that

h\circ k\circ j_{\overline{S},\,\overline{f}}=h\circ(\pi_{1}\iota_{\overline{(S,0,0)},\,\overline{(f,!)}})=j_{\overline{\pi_{1}(S,0,0)},\,\overline{\pi_{1}(f,!)}}=j_{\overline{S},\,\overline{f}}

for all
\overline{S:\mathsf{Set}_{0}}
and
\overline{f:\llbracket{\emptyset;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{1}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=S}]\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{1}\rho)}
, so that
h\circ k=\mathit{id}
.
To see that
k\circ h=\mathit{id}
we first observe that

k\circ h\circ(\pi_{1}\iota_{\overline{R},\,\overline{m}})=k\circ j_{\overline{\pi_{1}R},\,\overline{\pi_{1}m}}=\pi_{1}\iota_{\overline{(\pi_{1}R,0,0)},\,\overline{(\pi_{1}m,!)}}

(23)

for all
\overline{R:\mathsf{Rel}_{0}}
and
\overline{m:\llbracket{\emptyset;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho}
. Then note that, for each sequence of morphisms

\overline{(\mathit{id}_{\pi_{1}R},!):(\pi_{1}R,0,0)\to R}
, we have that

\overline{m\,\circ\,\llbracket{\emptyset;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathit{id}_{\rho}[\overline{\alpha:=(\mathit{id}_{\pi_{1}R},!)}]=(\pi_{1}m,!)}

in the indexing category for the colimit representation of the
codomain of
k
. This implies that

\iota_{\overline{R},\,\overline{m}}\,\circ\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathit{id}_{\rho}[\overline{\alpha:=(\mathit{id}_{\pi_{1}R},!)}]=\iota_{\overline{(\pi_{1}R,0,0)},\,\overline{(\pi_{1}m,!)}}

Projecting the
first component thus gives

(\pi_{1}\iota_{\overline{R},\,\overline{m}})\,\circ\,\pi_{1}(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathit{id}_{\rho}[\overline{\alpha:=(\mathit{id}_{\pi_{1}R},!)}])=\pi_{1}\iota_{\overline{(\pi_{1}R,0,0)},\,\overline{(\pi_{1}m,!)}}

Now, the induction hypothesis on
\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F
gives that

\begin{array}[]{lll}&&\pi_{1}(\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\mathit{id}_{\rho}[\overline{\alpha:=(\mathit{id}_{\pi_{1}R},!)}])\\
&=&\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\mathit{id}_{\pi_{1}\rho}[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{\pi_{1}R}}]\\
&=&\mathit{id}_{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{1}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=\pi_{1}R}]}\end{array}

so that, in fact,
\pi_{1}\iota_{\overline{R},\,\overline{m}}=\pi_{1}\iota_{\overline{(\pi_{1}R,0,0)},\,\overline{(\pi_{1}m,!)}}
. Finally, by
Equation
23
we have
k\circ h\circ(\pi_{1}\iota_{\overline{R},\,\overline{m}})=\pi_{1}\iota_{\overline{(\pi_{1}R,0,0)},\,\overline{(\pi_{1}m,!)}}=\pi_{1}\iota_{\overline{R},\,\overline{m}}
for all
\overline{R}
and
\overline{m}
, and thus
k\circ h=\mathit{id}
.

To prove Equation
21
, first recall that

\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f
is defined to be

\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{R}}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho^{\prime}}\\
\circ\;\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f}

and
\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash(\mathsf{Lan}^{\overline{\alpha}}_{\overline{K}}F)\overline{A}}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}f)
is defined to be

\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=S}]}}\,\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}f)[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{S}}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho^{\prime})}\\
\circ\;\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=S}]}}\,\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=S}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}(\pi_{i}f)}

It therefore suffices to show that

\pi_{i}\Big(\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{R}}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho^{\prime}}\Big)\\
=\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{1}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=S}]}}\,\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}f)[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{S}}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho^{\prime})}

(24)

and that

\pi_{i}\Big(\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f}\Big)\\
=\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=S}]}}\,\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=S}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}f)}

(25)

To prove Equation
24
we first observe that the
morphisms
\iota^{\prime}_{\overline{R},\overline{m}}\,\circ\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{R}}]
form a cocone for

\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho^{\prime}}

with vertex

\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho^{\prime}[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho^{\prime}}

The universal property (Equation
18
) of

\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{R}}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho^{\prime}}

therefore gives that, for all
\overline{R:\mathsf{Rel}_{0}}
and
\overline{m:\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho^{\prime}}
,

\begin{array}[]{lll}&&\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{R}}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho^{\prime}}\,\circ\,\iota_{\overline{R},\overline{m}}\\[8.61108pt]
&=&\iota^{\prime}_{\overline{R},\overline{m}}\,\circ\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{R}}]\end{array}

Here,
\iota
is the morphism mapping the cocone into the colimit

\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho^{\prime}}

and
\iota^{\prime}
is the morphism mapping the cocone into the colimit

\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho^{\prime}[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho^{\prime}}

Projecting, together with the induction hypothesis for
\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F
, thus gives

\begin{array}[]{lll}&&\pi_{i}\Big(\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{R}}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho^{\prime}}\Big)\,\circ\,\pi_{i}\iota_{\overline{R},\overline{m}}\\[8.61108pt]
&=&\pi_{i}\iota^{\prime}_{\overline{R},\overline{m}}\,\circ\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}f)[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{\pi_{i}R}}]\end{array}

Equation
22
and its analogue for

\iota^{\prime}
and
j^{\prime}
then give

\begin{array}[]{lll}&&\pi_{i}\Big(\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{R}}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho^{\prime}}\Big)\,\circ\,k\,\circ\,j_{\overline{\pi_{i}R},\overline{\pi_{i}m}}\\[8.61108pt]
&=&\pi_{i}\Big(\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{R}}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho^{\prime}}\Big)\,\circ\,k\,\circ\,h\,\circ\,\pi_{i}\iota_{\overline{R},\overline{m}}\\[8.61108pt]
&=&\pi_{i}\Big(\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{R}}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho^{\prime}}\Big)\,\circ\,\pi_{i}\iota_{\overline{R},\overline{m}}\\[8.61108pt]
&=&\pi_{i}\iota^{\prime}_{\overline{R},\overline{m}}\,\circ\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}f)[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{\pi_{i}R}}]\\[8.61108pt]
&=&k^{\prime}\,\circ\,h^{\prime}\,\circ\,\pi_{i}\iota^{\prime}_{\overline{R},\overline{m}}\,\circ\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}f)[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{\pi_{i}R}}]\\[8.61108pt]
&=&k^{\prime}\,\circ\,j^{\prime}_{\overline{\pi_{i}R},\overline{\pi_{i}m}}\,\circ\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}f)[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{\pi_{i}R}}]\end{array}

where
j
is the morphism mapping the cocone into the colimit

\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=S}]}}\,\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=S}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho^{\prime})}

and
j^{\prime}
is the morphism mapping the cocone into the colimit

\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=S}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho^{\prime})[\overline{\alpha:=S}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho^{\prime})}

Since both
k
and
k^{\prime}
are isomorphisms, we have that, up to
isomorphism,

\begin{array}[]{lll}&&\pi_{i}\Big(\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{R}}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho^{\prime}}\Big)\circ\,j_{\overline{\pi_{i}R},\overline{\pi_{i}m}}\\[8.61108pt]
&=&j^{\prime}_{\overline{\pi_{i}R},\overline{\pi_{i}m}}\,\circ\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}f)[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{\pi_{i}R}}]\end{array}

By the surjectivity of each
\pi_{i}:\mathsf{Rel}\to\mathsf{Set}
we have that

\begin{array}[]{lll}&&\pi_{i}\Big(\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{R}}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho^{\prime}}\Big)\,\circ\,j_{\overline{S},\overline{g}}\\[8.61108pt]
&=&j^{\prime}_{\overline{S},\overline{g}}\,\circ\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}f)[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{S}}]\end{array}

for all
\overline{S:\mathsf{Set}_{0}}
and
\overline{g:\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=S}]\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho^{\prime})}
. That is,

\pi_{i}\Big(\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{R}}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho^{\prime}}\Big)

satisfies the universal property (Equation
18
) of

\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{1}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=S}]}}\,\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}f)[\overline{\alpha:=\mathit{id}_{S}}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho^{\prime})}

The two expressions must therefore be equal, and thus
Equation
24
holds.

To prove Equation
25
we first observe that
the universal property (Equation
18
) of

\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f}

gives that,
for every
\overline{R:\mathsf{Rel}_{0}}
and
\overline{m:\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho}
,

\begin{array}[]{lll}&&\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f}\,\circ\,\iota_{\overline{R},\overline{m}}\\[8.61108pt]
&=&\iota^{\prime}_{\overline{R},\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f\,\circ\,m}}\end{array}

where
\iota
is the cocone into the colimit

\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho}

and
\iota^{\prime}
is the cocone into the colimit

\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho^{\prime}}

Projecting, together with the induction hypothesis for

\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F
, thus gives

\begin{array}[]{lll}&&\pi_{i}\Big(\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f}\Big)\,\circ\,\pi_{i}\iota_{\overline{R},\overline{m}}\\[8.61108pt]
&=&\pi_{i}\iota^{\prime}_{\overline{R},\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f\,\circ\,m}}\end{array}

Equation
22
and its analogue for

\iota^{\prime}
and
j^{\prime}
then give that, up to isomorphism,

\begin{array}[]{lll}&&\pi_{i}\Big(\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f}\Big)\,\circ\,j_{\overline{\pi_{i}R},\overline{\pi_{i}m}}\\[8.61108pt]
&=&j^{\prime}_{\overline{\pi_{i}R},\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}(\pi_{i}f)\,\circ\,\pi_{i}m}}\end{array}

where
j
is the cocone into the colimit

\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=S}]}}\,\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=S}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)}

and
j^{\prime}
is the cocone into the colimit

\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=S}]}}\,\lambda\overline{S}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=S}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}(\pi_{i}\rho^{\prime})}

By the surjectivity of each
\pi_{i}:\mathsf{Rel}\to\mathsf{Set}
we have that

\begin{array}[]{lll}&&\pi_{i}\Big(\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f}\Big)\,\circ\,j_{\overline{S},\overline{g}}\\[8.61108pt]
&=&j^{\prime}_{\overline{S},\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}(\pi_{i}f)\,\circ\,g}}\end{array}

for every
\overline{S:\mathsf{Set}_{0}}
and
\overline{g:\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)[\overline{\alpha:=S}]\to\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Set}}(\pi_{i}\rho)}
. That is,

\pi_{i}\Big(\big(\textit{Lan}_{\overline{\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\overline{\alpha}\vdash K}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]}}\,\lambda\overline{R}.\,\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi,\overline{\alpha}\vdash F}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}\rho[\overline{\alpha:=R}]\big)\,\overline{\llbracket{\Gamma;\Phi\vdash A}\rrbracket^{\mathsf{Rel}}f}\Big)

satisfies the universal property (Equation
18
) of
